# Session log — 2026-08

> Archived from CLAUDE.md on 2026-08-30. Newest first.
> This is the project's engineering changelog: what changed, why, what was
> verified, and what was deliberately not done. Durable rules extracted from
> these entries live in CLAUDE.md; this file is the detail behind them.
>
> **Section references.** Entries below were written when CLAUDE.md carried
> everything in numbered sections. On 2026-08-30 that file was split, so pointers
> to the sections that moved (§11, §13, §16–§33) were rewritten here to name the
> `docs/` file they now live in. Two things were deliberately left as written:
> low-numbered refs (§0–§9), whose meaning depends on how old the entry is — in a
> pre-split entry §4/§6/§7 mean build state / model registry / commands, and in a
> post-split one they mean conventions / config topology / hard-won lessons — and
> refs like §113 or §126c, which are SESSION numbers, not sections.

---
**Session summary (2026-08-30, session 154 — the models publish probabilities 6-16pp above what they deliver; a calibration layer, and the gate that never caught it):**
- Mike: "still too many live bets on MLB... provide EV thresholds and expected bets/units per week" → cuts set → "retrain it" → and then, as each measurement superseded the last, "do 1, 3 and 4": build the calibration layer, treat `mlb_moneyline` as its own decision, fix the go-live gate. **The retrain was dropped on evidence**, which is the story of the session.
- **THREE OF MY OWN DIAGNOSES WERE WRONG BEFORE ONE SURVIVED**, each killed by a larger sample, and the sequence is the point:
  1. *"The live model has never seen 2026."* True — the PBP corpus stopped at 2025 — but its 2026 point estimate is nearly unbiased (**−0.072 runs per state** over 75,313 holdout states). Not the cause.
  2. *"The whole gap is innings 1-3"* (32 settled bets: 50.0% against a claimed 69.7%). Contradicted by ~540,000 state-line observations showing the overconfidence is **uniform** across the game, +7 to +13pp at every inning.
  3. *"The Poisson tail is structurally wrong"* (overdispersion). Contradicted by the in-sample check: on 2022-24 the same pricing is **−2 to −3pp, i.e. slightly UNDER-confident**.
  4. **The survivor**: on 2025 — out of sample, and NOT 2026 — it is **+9 to +10pp**, almost identical to 2026. So it is a **generalisation failure**: a model fits its training seasons more tightly than any season it has not seen, and every live pick is made out of sample. That is the clean argument against retraining: it moves the boundary, not the behaviour, and 2027 would look exactly like 2025 and 2026 do now.
- **THE FINDING IS PORTFOLIO-WIDE, NOT ONE LIVE MODEL.** Graded through `mv_scored_pick_outcomes` at the probabilities actually bet: `mlb_prop_pitcher_hits` claims 65.8% and wins 49.6% (**+16.2pp**), `wnba_prop_player_points` +15.0, `wnba_prop_player_pra` +12.4, **`mlb_moneyline` +10.5**, `pitcher_outs` +10.1, `pitcher_walks` +10.0, `pitcher_er` +9.1, `f5_moneyline` +8.6, `wnba_rebounds` +8.4, `pitcher_k` +7.8, `wnba_assists` +6.5, `wnba_threes` +5.9 — and `mlb_prop_batter_rbi` **−6.0pp the other way**. Twelve over, one under. It tracks SAMPLE SIZE, not sport or market: the four best-calibrated models are the four with thousands of graded picks.
- **I applied §7's coverage traps to my own measurement before reporting it**, twice. The deleted-`NONE`-row window (06-26→08-08) moved every model by ≤1.5pp — the finding is not a coverage artifact. The **model-version trap** did matter: scoping each model to its own active version moved `batter_tb` +4.6→+1.2pp and `batter_hits` +3.2→+0.2pp, because a gap measured across a retrain describes a blend of the live model and its dead predecessor. Both corrections are baked into the fit window.
- **NEW `models/probability_calibration.py`** — Platt-on-logit maps, two parameters (several models have only a few hundred graded picks and the gap is smooth in confidence; isotonic would fit the tail), **symmetric** so a prop's over and under still sum to 1, scoped to the active version, documented contamination excluded (`mlb_over_under`'s NaN-line era, `mlb_runline`'s frozen-bullpen era), clean windows only, and **refusing entirely below 150 graded picks**.
- **A map is published only where it demonstrably helps on picks it was NOT fitted on** — fit the older half, measure the newer half, compare against leaving it raw. **Applied to 10**: pitcher_hits 12.9→1.3pp, wnba_points 10.7→1.5, batter_rbi 7.1→0.7, pitcher_er 7.4→1.2, pitcher_k 6.8→2.6, wnba_rebounds 6.0→1.8, plus threes/tb/walks/runs. **Refused for 7** where the map made the held-out half WORSE (mlb_moneyline 5.2→7.2, f5_ml, pitcher_outs, pitcher_walks, wnba_pra, wnba_assists, batter_hits) and **3 more for thin data**. Fitting a map and applying it anyway would trade a known bias for an unknown one.
- **PHASE 1 ONLY, and the arithmetic is why.** `picks.model_probability_cal` is stamped at score time (one choke point, `_insert_picks`, covering pre-game + MLB live + NCAAF live) and shown on pick detail. **The decision path is untouched** — `edge`, BET/AVOID, Kelly and every threshold still run on the raw number, because `mlb_moneyline`'s 0.72 cut maps to ~0.62 and applying calibration without re-cutting would take it from ~2 picks a week to **zero**. Every cut in `config.py` was swept on raw probabilities. This is the `best_line` precedent exactly. Phase 2 — re-sweep and flip — is a §1b model update needing a person's call. A test asserts the signal path never reads the calibrated field.
- **ITEM 3, `mlb_moneyline`: the answer is do nothing, and the tooling reached it without me.** At its OWN cut (≥0.72, 37 graded): **May 12–Jun 3 claims 75.1% and wins 52.6% (+22.4pp); Jun 3–Aug 28 claims 74.8% and wins 77.8% (−3.0pp)**. The entire gap is one stale three-week window, which is exactly why its map failed the transfer test and was refused. Applying a −10pp haircut would have understated a model that is currently over-delivering.
- **ITEM 4, the gate — and the defect is worse than "the metric is unweighted".** `_mean_calibration_error` averages bins **unweighted** (a 20-sample bin counts as much as a 5,000-sample one) and **across the whole probability range**, where the mass sits near 0.5 and the models are fine, so the bands that get bet are diluted away. But for Poisson models **it was not measuring a probability at all**: `_poisson_calibration_error` checks the COUNT fit, while the scorer bets the Poisson tail at the live line — a serve-time transformation training never evaluated. `mlb_live_total_runs` shipped on `calibration_score = 0.4846`, a runs-scale number that cannot be compared to a 5% gate.
- **The proof, on the artifact's own 2025 holdout (287,334 priced states): legacy 0.0882 FAIL, weighted 0.0961 FAIL, actionable 0.0975 FAIL.** The gate did not pass a bad number — it never computed one. Any probability-scale metric run in June would have stopped this shipping.
- **Fixes**: `cal_error_weighted` and `cal_error_actionable` (weighted, restricted to `p >=` the model's own threshold) added as **NEW** fields — `cal_error` deliberately untouched so historical `model_registry` rows keep their meaning. `poisson_probability_metrics()` prices each holdout state across candidate lines and gates the derived probability; wired into live Poisson training, logging an ERROR on failure. Verified to discriminate: true-Poisson data 0.0028, overdispersed 0.0911 — and the overdispersed figure lands on the real model's 0.0975, corroborating the mechanism.
- **Forward half**: `model_calibration` health check on the LIVE graded record (WARN 5pp, CRIT 8pp, n≥150, scoped to the active version), because a training-time metric cannot see drift after shipping. On its first run it immediately reported **8 models 8pp+ overconfident**. Dashboard gets the same per-model gap beside the performance rows, flagged with whether a map is published.
- **A live bug caught in my own new code, worth remembering**: the applied-map lookup was `payload LIKE '%"applied": true%'`. `data.db` passes params to psycopg2 whenever they are not None — **an empty tuple counts** — so a literal `%` becomes a format placeholder, the query raises, and `monitoring/store._rows` swallows it and returns `[]`. The dashboard silently reported every model as unmapped. Replaced with a real `applied` column; a test now forbids `LIKE` in both call sites.
- **Also fixed: `backfill_pbp` silently voided the rest of a run after any single game error** and still exited on a SUCCESS line. Postgres poisons a connection after a failed statement, and the handler never rolled back — a unique-key conflict around game 600 of 1,818 left the remaining ~2,200 schedule entries "processed" and nothing written, reported as `complete: 0/2869 games`. Rolls back per game, counts errors, refuses to call a zero-write run a success. Five tests, verified to fail against the unfixed source.
- **The 2026 PBP backfill completed (1,818 games / ~100k plays)** and is kept: it produced the 2025-vs-2026 diagnosis that killed the retrain, and it is the corpus any live calibration work needs.
- **Not done, deliberately**: the retrain (dropped on evidence, per mike's "do 1, 3, 4"); Phase 2 threshold re-sweep (needs a person's call); and no model shut down — every live model is ≥0 ROI and the samples are 22-54 bets, where a shutdown call would be exactly the noise-fitting §7 warns about.
- **Verification**: NEW `tests/test_probability_calibration.py` (20 — symmetry, monotonicity, upward maps, every refusal path, the version-era and contamination windows, the legacy metric left unchanged, the actionable metric seeing what the legacy one dilutes, the Poisson gate discriminating true-Poisson from overdispersed, stamping never raising, the decision path never reading the calibrated field, and the `LIKE` bug) + 5 in `tests/test_pbp_backfill_resilience.py`. Full suite **1,579 passed / 1 failed** — that one a pre-existing Windows bash/mktemp issue in `test_refresh_pass_parallel`, verified identical on `origin/master` in a worktree.

**Session summary (2026-08-30, session 153 — live cutoffs set, and a recalibration loop that re-derives them every pass and publishes to the dashboard):**
- Mike: "still too many live bets on MLB. suspect the same for NCAAF... provide EV thresholds and expected bets/units per week at each threshold and I will set a cutoff" → then "set prob 0.70 and EV 0.28, and flat 1u stake. cut the least bad one on ncaaf. also since the live picks and models move so fast, these should be machine learning models — as you get more data, be honest if this does not predict success and recalibrate and suggest new cutoffs. I want those cutoffs with projections and results published in the railway dash."
- **THE FINDING THAT CHANGED THE QUESTION: volume tripled on 2026-08-29 and no threshold moved.** Live MLB went from ~35% of games producing a bet (0-3/day) to **100% (8-9/day, ~63/week)**. Cause is the first-signal lock (session 133) meeting 5s polling (session 134): before, delete-and-replace meant only a lane's FINAL state survived, so a total that crossed and fell back left nothing; now the first crossing is captured and kept, and at 5s almost every game crosses. Mean model probability also rose 0.68 → 0.72, because the lock catches the most extreme moment rather than a surviving one. **The lifetime average (10 bets/week over 5.7 weeks) understated the live rate by 6x** — a threshold chosen off it is chosen for a world that no longer exists.
- **Two corrections I had to make to my own analysis before it was safe to act on**, both recorded because both would have produced a worse decision:
  1. **The EV floor was ALREADY 0.32.** My sweep table averaged 08-29 (pre-floor, EVs down to 0.178) with 08-30 (post-floor, every EV ≥ 0.320), so it understated where the floor sat and made 0.28 look like a tightening. It is a slight LOOSENING. Applied as instructed and flagged in `MODEL_MIN_EV`'s comment; on the only clean day it binds on nothing once prob ≥ 0.70 is applied (all five survivors are EV 0.320-0.351). One line to put back.
  2. **"Flat 1u stake" was already done.** `conviction_for()` has returned FLAT_CONVICTION = 1.0 for every pick since 2026-08-29, in both `tracking/discord_notifier.py` and mobile `thresholds.ts`. My "avg stake 3.47u" used the LEGACY kelly÷1% formula, so I overstated weekly exposure by ~3x (226u vs the real ~79u). Nothing to build; corrected in the reply.
- **Cuts set** (config, `model_action_thresholds` via `threshold_sync`, and the mobile fallback): `mlb_live_total_runs` prob 0.68 → **0.70** + EV **0.28**; `ncaaf_live_total` prob 0.62 → **0.66** + EV **0.22**; `ncaaf_live_win_prob` prob 0.58 → **0.66** + EV **0.22**. NCAAF is **least-bad and explicitly unvalidated** — 10 settled bets from ONE Saturday, and every EV cut on that sample is still negative overall. prob 0.66 + EV 0.22 keeps 3 of 8 totals bets (2-1) and is the only cell that loses least while keeping more than one bet.
- **NEW `config.LIVE_MAX_BETS_PER_WEEK` (30 / 20 / 10).** Not a runtime cap — nothing enforces it at score time. It is the constraint the recommender optimises UNDER, and it exists because the first unconstrained run of the sweep **recommended a LOOSER cut** (prob 0.66 / EV 0.22, 39.7 bets/wk) than the one it was checking (0.70 / 0.28, 28/wk). More bets at higher ROI is not an answer to "too many bets". Pinned by `test_the_volume_ceiling_actually_binds`, which asserts both branches.
- **NEW `tracking/live_calibration.py` — the ML-honesty loop.** Per live model it reports (1) **calibration**: mean predicted probability vs realised win rate; (2) **EV honesty**: mean predicted EV vs realised ROI; (3) a **prob × EV sweep** with a plateau score; (4) **cost per week**, projected from the RECENT regime. Today it says `mlb_live_total_runs` **claims 70.0% and wins 56.0% (14.0pp gap)** and **predicts +28.5% EV against +3.1% realised (25.4pp gap)** — i.e. overconfidence, which is a calibration error a threshold cannot repair, so the verdict leads with "retrain is the lever". NCAAF is worse (28.2pp and 30.0pp) on 8 and 2 bets.
- **The recommender is built to REFUSE**, which is the point: no cell with 15+ settled bets → "NOT ENOUGH DATA, leave the cutoff alone"; no profitable cell → "retrain or pause — shipping the least-bad cell would be fitting noise"; nothing inside the ceiling → says so. A recommender that always recommends something is one you cannot act on. It also prefers a plateau (≥4 of 8 neighbours positive) to a higher peak and labels a peak as a peak.
- **Published to the Railway dashboard** (Models → **Live tuning**): cutoff in force, projected bets+units/week against the ceiling, actual record, calibration gap, and the verdict, with the sweep's alternative underneath. Colour is narrow and deliberate — bets/week red **over the ceiling**, ROI **grey below 12 settled** whatever its sign (a 3-bet sample is a number, not a result), calibration gap red past 8pp. Wired as pipeline step `live-calibration` on the daily run **and every refresh pass** (after settle), reading the new `live_calibration` table; the dashboard never recomputes on a tick. Verified end-to-end through the real HTTP server.
- **`live_calibration` creates its own table at write time** (the `run_ledger` precedent — the Supabase MCP is read-only, and a feature needing a manual migration first does nothing until someone remembers) **and locks it down in the same breath**: `ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` + `REVOKE ALL FROM anon, authenticated` **by name**, because Postgres' default grants hand anon everything and a `REVOKE ... FROM PUBLIC` does nothing (CLAUDE.md §7). Verified live: RLS on, `anon_select` false, 3 rows.
- **Two source-of-truth bugs found and fixed in `ncaaf_live/serve.py`:** it carried its OWN copies of the prob/edge floors, so the file that DECIDES could disagree with the file the scorer, `threshold_sync`, the app's action filter and the record views all read — and it did (config said edge 0.08, serve said 0.12). And `MODEL_MIN_EV` was honoured by the MLB live scorer but not here, so an EV floor set for an NCAAF model was **silently inert**. serve.py now reads config with a guarded standalone fallback, and `_decide` applies the EV floor after prob/edge so it can only ever tighten, never on AVOID, never without a price.
- **THE TEST SUITE WAS UNRUNNABLE ON WINDOWS AND NOBODY KNEW.** A dozen tests assert things about the repo's own source by reading it back; `read_text()` with no encoding uses the platform default (cp1252), and the source is full of box-drawing characters. They did not fail, they raised `UnicodeDecodeError` — and `tests/test_pick_lock_is_on_picks.py` raised it at **COLLECTION** time, aborting the whole run. Since there is no CI on pull requests, `python -m pytest -q tests/` is the repo's only quality gate, and it was dead on the only machine that runs it. Added `encoding="utf-8"` across 11 test files. **Result: 14 failed + a collection abort → 1,456 passed, 0 failed.** Promoted to CLAUDE.md §7 verification standards.
- **Two stale tests de-hardcoded rather than re-pinned.** `test_discord_price_bound.py` carried the literal answer for the EV floor of the day (-106 at 0.32) and named `ncaaf_live_total` as its "model with no EV floor" — which acquired one here, so that test had quietly stopped testing what it says. Both now derive from config and assert the RULE (the tightest gate binds; the published bound must itself qualify), so they cannot rot the same way again.
- **Verification:** NEW `tests/test_live_calibration.py` **21 tests** (EV arithmetic, Wilson widening on small samples, push handling, both calibration gaps, all three recommender refusals, the ceiling binding in both directions, plateau-over-peak, projection from the regime, the prob-cut vs EV-cut selecting different picks, and the lockdown naming anon/authenticated rather than PUBLIC) + **5 NCAAF live tests** (floors come from config, EV floor wired, declines a short-priced qualifier, never gates AVOID, inert without a price). Full suite **1,456 passed / 0 failed**. Dashboard verified through a real `build_server` + `/api/snapshot` + `/` fetch. `threshold_sync` re-run (70 models). Mobile fallback edited (three numeric literals; `npx tsc` not runnable — no node_modules on this checkout).
- **Two things I did NOT do, deliberately:** no retrain (the calibration gap says that is the real lever, but it is a separate decision with its own evidence), and no runtime enforcement of the weekly ceiling — a cap is a guarantee and a threshold is only a hope about volume, but adding one now would hide whether the cut itself works.

**Session summary (2026-08-30, session 152 — Recent News on the prop screens: a newspaper icon, a sheet, and a provider seam):**
- Matt (screenshot of another betting app's RotoWire-powered sheet): *"Can we copy this set up? In the top right is recent news. If your on a prop player it should have a new icon on the top right to click on recent news for that player. Not sure what API we need for that information."* Branch `claude/prop-player-recent-news-icon-vhndxe`.
- **The API question, answered honestly.** The screenshot's "Powered by ROTOWIRE" is the whole reason its notes are one-per-player with a separate ANALYSIS paragraph: that is licensed syndication (RotoWire syndicates to 60+ partners including DK and FanDuel; ~250 player notes/day per major sport). We do not hold that licence, so the provider is a SETTING — `config.PLAYER_NEWS_PROVIDER` — and v1 ships on **ESPN's hidden API**: free, no key, the same host `injury_ingestor` already reads. What ESPN returns is ARTICLES (headline + summary + link) tagged with the athletes they are about, not per-player fantasy notes. Coverage is therefore good for a storyline player and thin for a middle reliever. Options priced out and written up in `docs/player_news.md`: RotoWire (ken@rotowire.com, syndication), RotoBaller (advertises low-cost news feeds/APIs, 50–150 notes/day), SportsDataIO (`News`/`NewsByDate`/`NewsByPlayerID`, commercial agreement). **No spend was committed — that is Matt's call (§1b).**
- **What shipped, end to end.** New `player_news` table (`data/migrations/add_player_news.sql`, registered in `data/view_migrations.py` so it self-applies on the first pass after merge — the Supabase MCP here is read-only). New `data/ingestors/player_news_ingestor.py` with a `PROVIDERS` registry and an ESPN provider. New pipeline steps `player-news` (6am, step 5b2) and `player-news-refresh` — the latter placed in GROUP 1 of the parallelised refresh pass (session 150's `par` groups), since it is an independent network-bound producer nothing downstream reads. App side: `fetchPlayerNews`, `usePlayerNews`, `PlayerNewsButton` (the top-right icon + green freshness dot) and `PlayerNewsSheet` (date pill, headline, body, ANALYSIS block, "Powered by X", Close) wired into **both** `PlayerStatsScreen` and `PickDetailScreen`'s prop path — one component so the two cannot drift.
- **Storage is one row per (item, player), keyed twice.** `player_id` is ours; `player_key` is `normalize_player_name(...)`. That second key is session 148's lesson applied before it could bite again — the app prefers the id and falls back to the fold, and a name two players share resolves to **no id** rather than the wrong one. The TS mirror of the fold is pinned by a derived test that reads `data/name_match._SUFFIXES` and the folded character classes out of the Python and asserts they appear in `mobile/src/lib/playerNews.ts`.
- **The ESPN call budget is bounded on purpose.** ESPN has IP-blocked this worker twice (sessions 112, 115). One league feed per sport per run, plus at most `PLAYER_NEWS_MAX_TEAM_FETCHES` (12) team feeds spent only on teams with a player-prop pick today; the intraday step is gated on `REFRESH_PLAYER_NEWS_MAX_AGE_MIN` (60) — a MAX AGE, not a cadence, the same shape as the injury and weather refreshes — so ~42 passes a day cannot become 42 sweeps. Retention 21 days; this table is a cache of a feed we can re-read, so pruning it is safe under §1b.
- **The icon hides itself when the player has no notes.** An icon that opens an empty sheet teaches people not to tap it, and with a free provider empty is a genuine and common outcome. The green dot marks a note inside 48 hours — the one that might actually change a bet.
- **Verified:** `tests/test_player_news_ingestor.py` (22 cases — parsing, the dropped no-athlete article, the accented-name resolution, the ambiguity refusal, the call-budget cap, dedupe across feeds, quiet failure on a dead feed, the freshness gate, the unknown-provider refusal, the TS-mirror drift check). Full suite run below. Mobile `tsc --noEmit` error set is **byte-identical to `origin/master`** once line-number shifts are normalized — 27 errors, all of them the documented pre-existing `queries.ts` cast set, **0 in any file touched here** (compared against a `git worktree --detach origin/master`, not a stash).
- **Deliberately NOT done:** no paid feed was signed up for, so no ANALYSIS paragraphs exist yet — the block is built and stays hidden until a provider that carries one is configured. No push notification on a breaking note (that is a separate product call, and the notifier ledger rules in `docs/push_notifications.md` apply). NHL and NCAAF are configured out of `PLAYER_NEWS_SPORTS` by default because neither has a player detail screen to open the sheet from. Nothing about how any model prices or fires changed, so no `Updated-By:` trailer (§1b).

**Session summary (2026-08-30, session 151 — the custom-model builder stopped putting our numbers in the user's mouth, and the card leads with bets instead of filters):**
- Matt (screenshot of **New model** showing `MLB · Batter Hits` pre-filled with 78% / 17%): *"1. Under create custom model. Dont repopulate with amounts. Let the user add their own value or set it to any. 2. When you create the model it adds all the filters to the card which I don't like. Remove that and make the user add a title for the model and it also should populate with bets. If a user sets that, we should scan the DB and see the bets that fall under that set up by the user and how well of a model it is."* Branch `claude/custom-model-creation-fqefc5`.
- **What the 78/17 actually was: our own live cut, presented as the user's choice.** `addRule` seeded every new bet type from `ACTION_THRESHOLDS[modelId]` — for `mlb_prop_batter_hits` that is exactly `{min_prob: 0.78, min_edge: 0.17}`, and the footer's "80 graded picks · 59-21 · +9.5%" in the screenshot is the built-in model's own record. A user who tapped Create without touching a field saved a clone of the in-house model under their own name. Now a new bet type carries **no floors at all** and every field renders the "Any" placeholder, so a saved number is always one they typed.
- **Nothing needed to change server-side, and that was verified against production rather than assumed.** `custom_model_backtest` / `custom_model_picks` already `COALESCE((x->>'min_prob'), 0)` and `COALESCE((x->>'min_edge'), -9.99)`, so an omitted or explicitly-null floor is no floor. Live check on `mlb_prop_batter_hits`: bare `{"model_id":...}` and `{"min_prob":null,"min_edge":null}` both grade the **identical 20,677-pick universe (10,337-10,340, −6.49%)**; the seeded 78/17 grades **80 picks, 59-21, +9.53%** (the screenshot's numbers); prob-only 78 grades 409. The three shapes are distinct and correct.
- **`min_prob` / `min_edge` are now optional on `CustomModelRule`**, matching `min_ev`. `pickMatchesModel` skips an absent floor; `cleanRules` drops every blank key so the RPC payload stays minimal and a rule saved as "Any" is byte-identical to what an older build would send for the same criteria. Models saved before today still carry both floors and are untouched — this is additive, no migration.
- **The card now leads with the bets, not the filters.** `CustomModelRow` dropped the `describeFilters` chip wall (and the "N filters" subtitle) for the bet types it plays plus a **QUALIFYING BETS** block: the model matched against today's board (`useTodayPicks` + the same `pickMatchesModel` the backtest uses), up to three labelled with their DK price and a "+N more". Empty is stated as empty ("Nothing on the board qualifies right now") rather than being inferred from a wall of chips. The settled backtest row (Picks / Win % / Flat ROI / P&L) is unchanged — that is the "how well of a model it is" half, and it was already scanning the whole graded universe.
- **A title is now required.** `create()` silently fell back to `'Untitled model'`; the name is the model's only identity on the list, its detail header and the backtest card, so Create/Save is disabled (40% opacity) until a non-blank name AND at least one bet type exist, with an alert naming which is missing. The name is trimmed on save.
- **Filters were NOT removed from the builder** — only from the card. They still narrow a model and still show on its detail screen; Matt's objection was the card, and dropping the feature would have been a scope change nobody asked for. Flagged here in case he meant the whole thing.
- **Verification.** `verify_custom_model_filters.ts` extended with a new §7e (6 checks) pinning the absent-floor semantics — a bare rule matching on bet type alone, an explicit-null floor, each floor biting alone with the other unconstrained, the bet type still gating, and filters still ANDing over a bare rule — **all pass** alongside the existing suite. `npx tsc --noEmit` error set **byte-identical to `origin/master`** via a `git worktree --detach` baseline (57 pre-existing `queries.ts` cast errors, **0 in touched files**); deps installed with `npm ci` first, since without `node_modules` the run is nothing but TS2307. Full pytest **1,371 passed** (mobile-only diff, gate run anyway).
- **Not a model update** under §1b — this is the mobile custom-model builder, not a registered model's threshold, registry version, feature list or pause state. No `Updated-By:` trailer.
- **Outstanding for Matt:** JS-only, so it ships over the air on merge to master; force-quit and relaunch twice to pick it up.

**Session summary (2026-08-30, session 150 — NCAAF gets a player leaderboard: the CFBD box score the QB pull was already fetching and throwing away):**
- Ask: *"Add players for NCAAF stats page."* Branch `claude/ncaaf-stats-players-w00yc2`.
- **The data was already being fetched.** `ingest_ncaaf_qb_log` pulls `/games/players` per week and keeps only the passers; every rusher, receiver and defender in that payload was parsed away. So the leaderboard cost **zero extra CFBD calls**: `ingest_ncaaf_player_logs` does one fetch and writes both tables, and `ingest_ncaaf_qb_log` survives as a thin wrapper (the QB backfill and its tests keep working). `--backfill-players START END` fills history.
- **`ncaaf_player_game_log` does NOT replace `ncaaf_qb_game`.** They answer different questions off the same bytes: the QB table is the modelling substrate (one row per PASSER, `is_primary` = most attempts, FK'd by 2015-2025 training rows), the new one is display only — one row per PLAYER, every category, read by nothing but the app. `tests/test_ncaaf_player_stats_ingestor.py` pins that the two parsers agree on the passer's line off one payload, which is the whole reason the fetch is shared.
- **Three parser decisions, each of which is silent when wrong.** (1) ONE ROW PER PLAYER — CFBD lists a running quarterback under both `passing` and `rushing`, and a row per category would double-count him on the board and split his game log in half. (2) The **all-zero skip**: kickers, punters and returners appear only in categories this table does not store, and kept they would go 12-for-12 on an "at most N yards" board and bury the players actually in that market (the NFL leaderboard lesson, `isStatParticipant`, now football-wide rather than NFL-only). (3) A defender's INT comes from the `interceptions` category, never the `passing` INT, which is a pick THROWN — collapsing them would credit every quarterback with takeaways. Tackles/TFL/sacks are NUMERIC because college box scores charge shared tackles in halves.
- **No position column, on purpose.** CFBD's box score names participants, not positions, so the board groups by STAT (a passing board is whoever threw) rather than by a position we would have to guess. The NCAAF subtitle is therefore team-only where the NFL's carries `pos`.
- **Column names match `nfl_player_game_log` wherever the two sports share a stat**, so one mobile catalog key means one thing in both football leagues — with one guard: `propModelForStat` returns null for every NCAAF stat, because college props are not modelled and a shared `passing_yards` key must never resolve an "Add to play" button to an NFL model. No `targets` (CFBD does not report them).
- **Mobile wiring is the NFL path, reused, not a second one.** `nflSeasonCandidates` became `footballSeasonCandidates` (both leagues label a season by the fall it starts, so the calendar year the Stats screen passes is empty in the off-season and falls back a year); all four fetchers (`fetchSeasonTotals`, `fetchWindowTotals`, `fetchRecentGames`, `fetchSeasonStatValues`) take one `NFL || NCAAF` branch each; `playerLog` gained the table, its column list, the shared `FOOTBALL` set for the weekly cadence (25 rows, L3/L5/L10 windows) and the derived `rush_rec_tds`. NCAAF now supports Hit Rate mode and the player detail screen.
- **DB:** `add_ncaaf_player_stats_leaderboard.sql` — table + `v_player_season_totals_ncaaf` + the three RPCs, applied to Supabase 2026-08-30, all four surfaces smoke-tested against production on a season-1900 fixture that was then deleted (season totals, an L1 window, newest-first recent games, the stat-values array, and an unknown stat key returning zero rows rather than an error).
- **A grant gap found while doing it, and fixed:** default privileges hand `anon` ALL on anything new in `public`, and `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW` re-applies them — so the revoke has to come AFTER the view exists. `v_player_season_totals_nfl` and `nfl_player_game_log` were in exactly that state (anon holding INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE at the grant level, blocked only by RLS carrying no write policy); both are now SELECT-only alongside the new NCAAF pair. `get_advisors(security)` after the migration shows nothing new — the new table has its policy, the view and RPCs are security-invoker.
- **Not a model update** under §1b: no threshold, registry, feature list or pause changed, and no model reads the new table. No `Updated-By:` trailer.
- **Verification.** Full pytest **1,383 passed** (1,371 on `origin/master` + 12 new NCAAF parser tests). `npx tsc --noEmit` error set **byte-identical to `origin/master`** via a `git worktree --detach` baseline — 57 pre-existing `queries.ts` cast errors, 0 in touched files. `verify_ncaaf_ui.ts`, `verify_player_log.ts`, `verify_stats_board.ts`, `verify_hit_rate.ts` and `verify_team_board.ts` all PASS; the first three carry new NCAAF assertions (the old ones asserted NCAAF had no leaderboard — a stale assertion is worse than none).
- **Outstanding for Matt:** the daily pipeline fills the CURRENT season on its own after deploy (`step_ncaaf_stats`), so the board populates from Week 1 without any manual step. History needs one run — `python -m data.ingestors.cfbd_ingestor --backfill-players 2015 2025` on the worker or your machine (~17 free CFBD calls per season, ~50K rows per season) — until then the Stats tab shows its honest "No NCAAF Pass Yards data for this season yet" empty state for past years.

**Session summary (2026-08-30, session 155 — the iOS build that never started: an EAS quota refusal reported as a bare "exit code 1"):**
- Matt pasted the failed `Mobile TestFlight build` run log with no question attached. Branch `claude/ios-build-quota-nfjfdu`.
- **The cause is not in the code.** EAS refused the build outright: *"This account has used its iOS builds from the Free plan this month, which will reset in 1 day, 5 hours (on Tue Sep 01 2026)."* Credentials resolved, the archive uploaded, the fingerprint computed — everything up to the refusal succeeded, which is exactly why the run reads as healthy until it stops. No IPA was produced and nothing reached Apple.
- **The reportable bug is the reporting.** `eas build --json` puts the result on stdout and everything human on stderr; the step redirected stdout to `build_output.json` and let stderr fall through, so the job's only verdict was `Process completed with exit code 1` with the real line ~30 lines up the log. A quota refusal and a Fastlane compile failure were indistinguishable from the run's status.
- **What changed.** The build step now captures stderr (still streaming to the job log through `tee`, so a 25-minute build keeps its progress), strips ANSI, and classifies the failure as `quota` or `other`. A new "Post failure note on build failure" step writes the verdict into the same **Latest TestFlight build** tracking issue the success and submit-failure paths already use, and both cases get a `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY` block so the reason is on the run page rather than in the log. The quota body names the three ways forward in order: ship it over the air if it is JS-only, wait for the reset, or upgrade — the last flagged as Matt's spend decision, per §1b, not something CI suggests as routine.
- **A `set -e` trap, caught by testing rather than by reading.** The first version was `{ eas build ... ; echo $? > build_status; } 2>&1 | tee`. GitHub runs steps under `bash -e`, so the subshell died the instant `eas` exited non-zero and `echo $?` never ran — the classifier then read a file that did not exist and the step failed with `cat: build_status: No such file`. Fixed with `set +e` inside the group and a `|| echo 1` fallback on the read.
- **Verified by replaying the real log through the extracted step**: a stub `eas` reproducing the 2026-08-30 stderr verbatim (ANSI included) → `failure_kind=quota` with the reset sentence extracted; a Fastlane-style failure → `failure_kind=other`; success → `build_id` + `build_page_url` as before; exit 0 with an empty `--json` array → a clean `other` failure instead of a `null` build id reaching `eas submit`. The github-script bodies were rendered against stubbed `github`/`context`/`core` and read back.
- **Two stale statements in `mobile/TESTFLIGHT.md` §5 corrected in passing**, both actively misleading: "GitHub Actions was removed from this repo" (restored 2026-08-25 — the doc still told you to run the commands by hand) and "the `production` profile in `eas.json` has `autoIncrement: true`" (it does not, and the workflow stamps the number from the run number precisely because EAS's auto-increment never persisted — following the doc and building locally is how you get an Apple duplicate-build-number rejection).
- **Deliberately NOT done, and why.** (1) **No `.easignore`**, despite EAS flagging the 253 MB archive in the same log: eas-cli archives the whole repo because `mobile/` sits inside one, and an `.easignore` REPLACES `.gitignore` for archiving rather than adding to it, so a wrong one makes the archive bigger or breaks the build. It costs upload time, not builds, and the allowance is spent — a failed experiment now costs a real build. Noted in `docs/local_ops.md` with what a correct one would have to re-list. (2) **No pre-flight quota check**: EAS refuses only after the upload, and the remaining-builds figure is not in a documented API surface, so a check would be a guess at an untestable GraphQL schema. (3) **No plan upgrade** — Matt's call; per Expo's published pricing the Free plan is 15 iOS builds/month and Starter is ~$19/mo, to be confirmed at https://expo.dev/pricing rather than taken from here.
- **No model, threshold or pick behaviour was touched**, so no `Updated-By` trailer (§1b: cadence and plumbing changes do not carry one).

**Session summary (2026-08-30, session 148 — the accented-name prop gap: every José, Acuña and Hernández was skipped by every priced prop market):**
- Matt (screenshot of the MLB Stats board, 1+ Home Runs, L10, Playing today): *"This is common with the stat pages why are we missing so many lines for players"* — seven of the eight visible players showed "—" in the ODDS column. Branch `claude/stat-pages-missing-lines-vuddzo`.
- **Two independent causes, measured in production before touching code.** The board's ODDS pill is fed ONLY by `todayPicks` (`StatsScreen.oddsByPlayer` matches `pick.model_id === propModelForStat(stat)` on `player_id`), so a player the model never scored has no price to show even when DraftKings is pricing him.
  1. **Lineups (expected, time-of-day).** Prop scoring reads `lineup_slots ... is_confirmed = TRUE` (`prop_feature_engine.build_batter_scoring_rows`). At 12:24pm ET only 10 of the day's 14 games had lineups; the four late games (HOU@NYM, BAL@OAK, PHI@LAA, CIN@CHC) had **zero** picks against 18–19 DK-priced batters each. That is exactly the set of dashes in Matt's screenshot — Torrens, Vázquez (NYM), Alonso (BAL), Bregman, Conforto (CHC) — and it fills in on the hourly refresh as lineups post. Not a bug.
  2. **The real bug: an exact-string name join.** `scorer._get_prop_dk_odds` matched `player_prop_odds.player_name` verbatim, on a comment asserting "both sources use the MLB API's canonical full name". False: the roster feeds accent names ("José Ramírez"), the Odds API writes them flat ("Jose Ramirez"). Every accented player therefore missed his own DK price. For PROB_ONLY models (HR) that wrote a row with NULL pricing; for every OTHER prop market the code `continue`d and **no row existed at all**.
- **The size of it.** 2026-08-30: 22 of the 171 confirmed batters carried a non-ASCII name, and DK priced all 22 — 20 of them had a home-run price and a NULL-priced pick, and only 2 of the 22 got a `mlb_prop_batter_hits` pick. August 2026: **16 of 570 accented lineup slots (2.8%) were ever scored, against 3,580 of 6,108 plain-ASCII slots (58.6%)**. Lifetime: **521 of 127,708 priced MLB prop picks (0.41%) carry an accented name** where accented names are ~9% of a slate. José Ramírez, Ronald Acuña Jr., Julio Rodríguez, Teoscar Hernández, Yandy Díaz — none of them have been priced, ever.
- **The fix is one shared helper and one shared lookup, per §1b.** New `data/name_match.py`: `normalize_player_name` folds ONLY what cannot distinguish two people (diacritics, case, apostrophes/periods, hyphens→space, trailing generational suffix, whitespace) — never truncation, initials or fuzzy matching. `resolve_feed_name` returns the feed's spelling, or **None when two candidates fold alike** (a "Luis Garcia" / "Luis Garcia Jr." pair): a wrong price on the wrong player is worse than no pick. `_get_prop_dk_odds` keeps the exact match as its indexed fast path and only falls back for a miss, scoped to that game+market+DraftKings. All five prop lanes (MLB batter, WNBA, NBA, NFL, MLB pitcher) call that one function, so the fix lands in every sport at once.
- **Line shopping follows the resolved spelling.** `_get_prop_dk_odds` now returns the feed's `player_name`, and all five `_best_ctx` sites key `_best_prop_price` on it — it queries the same table, so handing it the roster's accented name would have produced a priced pick with no best-line row.
- **Verified against production names, not fixtures:** replayed the real DK candidate lists for all 22 of today's accented batters through `resolve_feed_name` — **22/22 resolve**, including the two a bare `unaccent()` misses ("Rafael Flores Jr." → "Rafael Flores", "Jonny DeLuca" → "Jonny Deluca"). New `tests/test_prop_name_match.py` (20 cases) pins the fold, the fast path, the fallback, the ambiguity refusal, and — derived from the source — that all five lanes use the resolved name. Full suite **1,347 passed** (1,327 on `origin/master` + the 20 new).
- **§1c is not touched.** Today's already-written NULL-priced HR rows stay exactly as they are: they were a pick at that moment and the first-signal lock keeps them. The markets that wrote no row (hits, RBI, runs, TB, walks, and the pitcher lanes) are not locked, so those players get picks on the next refresh pass after deploy; HR and anything already locked start clean tomorrow.
- **Deliberately NOT done** (Matt chose the name fix alone from four options): the Stats board still reads only `picks` for its ODDS column, so a player DK prices but no model has scored still shows "—" — including every player in a game whose lineup has not posted. Making the pill fall back to `v_latest_prop_odds_all_books` needs a product call on the tap target (the odds sheet and add-to-betslip both want a real `EnrichedPick`). Also left: the pill shows the model's own line as an `o0.5` subtitle regardless of where the ruler sits, so "2+ Hits" can display a 0.5 price. Both are logged here, unfixed.
- **`scripts/nfl_prop_market_card.py` is out of scope** — it prices off a live board fetch it keys itself (`quotes`), not this DB join, so it never had the gap.
- **Model update under §1b** — it changes which players the prop models fire on (~9% more per slate). Commit carries `Updated-By: matt`.
- **Housekeeping:** `create extension if not exists unaccent` was run against production while measuring the gap. It is additive, it may well have already been installed, and **the shipped code does not use it** (the fold is Python-side, so there is one implementation of the rule). Say the word and it comes back out.

**Session summary (2026-08-30, session 147 — "cards show DK, should be dynamic" (3rd ask): audited already-shipped, saved parlays book-aware, card's book stat opens the picker):**
- Matt (screenshot of the MLB board): *"In the cards it show DK and the betting line. This should be dynamic. If a user select Fandule it should show the betting lines for that bet with FD and this should be the case for all our lines. And you should be able to place bet directly with FD."* Branch `claude/dynamic-betting-lines-sportsbook-y3uonk`.
- **Audit first: the ask is ALREADY SHIPPED and already on Matt's phone.** This is the third time this ask has arrived (sessions 108 → 125 → 126); sessions 108–134 built it end-to-end: `usePreferredBook` + `SportsbookPickerSheet` (Settings, and tap the "Prices & bets at DraftKings ›" indicator on the Picks/Live headers) → `displayQuoteForPick` swaps the card's price stat to the chosen book (own line shown when it differs, labeled DK fallback when the book doesn't price the bet) → the green button becomes "Bet on FanDuel" with FD's own pre-filled betslip deep link. His screenshot INCLUDES the session-126 indicator, so the installed build has the feature — the cards read DK because the selected book is DK (the default). Verified live in Supabase, not just in code: today's board has FanDuel prices AND betslip links for the game markets and props checked (e.g. Scherzer `pitcher_strikeouts` 4.5 u−142 with `under_link`; F5/runline/WNBA ML rows all linked).
- **The one surface still hard-coded was saved parlays** — `SavedParlaysScreen` always said "Bet on DraftKings" with DK-only links, because a `SavedParlayLeg` snapshot deliberately carried nothing but `dkBetLink`. Fixed by snapshotting per-book single-leg links at save time (`SavedParlayLeg.bookLinks`, keyed by bookmaker; key present = that book priced the leg, value = its link or null) and a new pure `savedHandoffBookFor` mirroring the live betslip's honesty rule: hand off at the user's book only when the snapshot shows it priced EVERY real leg, else DraftKings; custom legs are book-agnostic and never disqualify; pre-upgrade saves (no `bookLinks`) keep handing off at DK. The button's label/colors now follow the book it actually opens.
- **Discoverability, the real reason this keeps coming back: the card's book stat is now the switch.** The "DK +140" column on every pick card (Picks, UFC/NFL, Live — all boards share `PickCard`) is tappable with a small chevron and opens the sportsbook picker directly, so the exact thing Matt keeps pointing at answers itself. Third ask = the Settings entry point and the header indicator weren't landing.
- **Verification:** `verify_betslip.ts` extended with 8 `savedHandoffBookFor`/`toSavedParlay` pins — 51/51 PASS (was 43); `verify_preferred_book` 54/54, `verify_line_shop`, `verify_slip_stability` all pass; `npx tsc --noEmit` error set byte-identical to the 28-error master baseline (HEAD == origin/master at branch time), 0 in touched files; full pytest 1,304 passed (mobile-only change, gate run anyway).
- **Not a model update** under §1b — display and hand-off only; the decision path still prices exclusively against DraftKings (`tests/test_multi_book_odds.py` untouched). No `Updated-By` trailer.
- **Outstanding for Matt:** JS-only mobile change → after merge run, from `mobile/`: `eas update --channel production --message "saved parlays follow your sportsbook; tap a card's book stat to switch"`, then force-quit + relaunch twice (the session-126 lesson: merged ≠ delivered).

**Session summary (2026-08-30, session 146 — the Picks board sorts by where the public is, and the card prints the number):**
- Matt, on a screenshot of the Picks board: *"Remove conference and replace with public to show where the public is betting."* The chip he meant is **Conf** (confidence), not conference. Branch `claude/conference-to-public-betting-qupybd`, merged as `a5b2c81` (#318).
- **What Conf was worth: nothing the eye wasn't already getting.** It sorted by `confidence_tier`, which every card already prints as a HIGH/MED/LOW pill — a sort that re-orders the board by a column you can read straight off it. Replaced with **Public**: `SortKey` `'conf'` → `'public'`, ordering by `public_bet_pct` (the share of public tickets on each pick's OWN side) descending, so the top of the board is where the crowd's money actually sits.
- **Direction is a judgement call, flagged not buried.** Descending is the literal read of "where the public is betting", but it puts the *caution* picks on top — the app's own thesis (`PublicBettingCard`, the Sharp Score's contrarian component) is that value sits on the light side. Called out in the PR body as a one-line flip; Matt merged as-is, so descending stands.
- **The change that made the sort legible was the one that wasn't asked for.** `contrarianTag()` only speaks on a **BET** sitting in a decisive band (≤40% or ≥65%), so a board ranked by public share would have shown almost nothing explaining its own order. Measured before writing anything: over the trailing 7 days, **400 of the 404 captured splits sit on NONE and AVOID rows**, and 64 of them are in the silent 41–64% middle band. A sort chip ranking cards by a number the cards never print is a chip that looks broken.
- **So the reading was split in two rather than widening the tag.** NEW `publicSplit()` — the raw crowd position for ANY signal type, middle band returned as `'split'` instead of `null`, money share carried through. `contrarianTag()` is now the judged, BET-only view derived from it, so the 40/65 bands live in one place. **The Sharp Score is untouched**: same bands, same neutral scoring for a split crowd, every pre-existing assertion green without edits.
- **On the card:** the green *"Sharp side"* / amber *"Public-heavy"* callout keeps every case it already owned; anything else carrying a split now gets a neutral grey *"62% public on this side"* — the number, no verdict. Deliberately not a second judged chip: the board already caps hero chips at two, and a third opinionated line would drown the differentiating signals.
- **Coverage is the caveat worth stating plainly.** Splits are Action Network consensus on full-game markets only: `mlb_over_under` (181/181), `mlb_moneyline` (172/172) and `mlb_runline` (51/51) carry them on **100%** of rows; props, F5, golf and every other sport store NULL across ~18,200 picks. No-split rows sort last, so on a prop-heavy board Public degrades to plain edge order rather than interleaving rows by a number that isn't there. **This is an MLB-game-lines feature today** — if the other sports should have it, that is an ingestor question, not a UI one.
- **Not a model update** (§1b): no threshold, registry, feature list or pause changed — a sort order and a card row. No `Updated-By:` trailer.
- **Verification.** `verify_sharp_score.ts` **26/26 pass**, nine of them new: five pin `publicSplit`'s bands, its signal-type independence, its null case and the money share; four pin the public sort order, the nulls-last rule and the edge tiebreak. `verify_custom_model_filters.ts` passes (it exercises the `public_bet_pct` filters). `npx tsc --noEmit` error set **byte-identical to `origin/master`** via a `git worktree --detach` baseline — 57 pre-existing `queries.ts` cast errors, **0 in touched files**. No Python changed, so pytest is untouched by the diff. JS-only, so it shipped over the air on merge.
- **One process note worth keeping.** The first `tsc` baseline diff read as a real regression ("and 40 more" vs "and 38 more" missing `Pick` properties) and was **not** — local `master` was one commit behind `origin/master`, and #316 had widened the `Pick` type. §7 already says local master is not a baseline; this is what the false alarm looks like when you follow the rule and re-base anyway.

**Session summary (2026-08-30, session 145 — one membership, two surfaces: App Store auth/billing + Discord linking for App Store launch):**
- Matt, with two screenshots of a competitor app: *"We need to add authentication to prepare for this app to go live on the App Store... Mirror this set up for how to pay for a membership and for a user to connect discord. We also need to make sure the email for the app is linked to give them access to discord and vice versa. If someone has discord they should be able to get an account on the app at no additional cost because they will pay via discord. If access is removed from one, it should be removed from the other."* Branch `claude/app-store-auth-discord-link-ww0tbw`.
- **Auth and billing were already built and dark** (sessions ~120s): Supabase email-OTP + Apple + Google, RevenueCat IAP rail, `subscriptions` table, paywall, both behind `AUTH_ENABLED` / `BILLING_ENABLED`. Nothing needed rebuilding. What was missing was the Discord half and the cross-entitlement.
- **Decisions taken to Matt before building** (three, answered): Discord memberships are sold through **Whop**; linking is an **explicit Discord OAuth link** (auto-matching only on verified emails); the ladder mirrors the reference — **Weekly / Monthly / Annual**.
- **The one idea the whole design rests on: the Discord ROLE is the entitlement carrier, and each side revokes only the role IT granted.** Two roles — ours (`DISCORD_APP_ROLE_ID`, granted when an app subscription entitles) and Whop's (granted on a Discord purchase). We never touch Whop's, Whop never touches ours. A single shared role would have meant cancelling either side stripped a member who had paid for the other; the split is what makes "remove from one, remove from the other" correct rather than destructive. `discord_links.app_role_granted` records that ours is on, so revocation is a fact we hold, not a guess about the guild's state.
- **Access is COMPUTED on read, never cached as a grant.** `public.my_access()` ORs `subscriptions` and `whop_memberships`, so a cancelled Whop membership loses app access on the very next read — no second table to keep in step, no window where a stale entitlement stands. Three match keys, all evidence the user controls the identity: the explicitly linked Discord id, the app account's `email_confirmed_at` email, and the linked account's Discord-**verified** email. An unverified address matches nothing — trusting one would let anybody claim a paid membership by typing the buyer's address.
- **Schema** (`add_discord_link_and_whop_memberships.sql`, 31 statements, validated against the real PostgreSQL grammar via `pglast`): `discord_links` (UNIQUE on `discord_user_id` — the anti-sharing rule; without it one paying member could hand free access to unlimited app accounts) and `whop_memberships` (keyed on the Whop membership id, NOT a Discord id, because a membership exists from the moment it is paid for — before the buyer connects Discord or ever signs in). `my_access_for(uuid)` is service-role only; `my_access()` is the `auth.uid()`-pinned wrapper the app gets, which is what stops it enumerating who is subscribed. Anon and authenticated REVOKEd **by name** per §7; service_role granted explicitly rather than relying on the default privileges the revokes strip. **NOT YET APPLIED — Matt runs it.**
- **Edge functions**: `discord-link` (verify_jwt **true** — a link asserts who someone is; HMAC-signed state binds the browser round trip to the caller; `guilds.join` makes joining one tap instead of an invite link), `whop-webhook` (HMAC over the **raw** body, and it deliberately touches no roles), `_shared/discord.ts`, `_shared/entitlement.ts`. `revenuecat-webhook` now syncs the role after its upsert — never throwing, because the subscription row is already written and failing the response would make the store retry a successful upsert; failures land in `discord_links.last_sync_error`.
- **Mobile**: `discordConfig` / `discordHelpers` / `discord.ts`, `useAccess`, `useEntitlement`, `DiscordLinkModal`, Settings connect/disconnect row, and the paywall rebuilt to the reference layout (three side-by-side plans, Restore Purchases, Redeem Code via StoreKit's offer-code sheet, Terms/Privacy/EULA row). All pure JS — ships over OTA, unlike IAP.
- **`useEntitlement()`, not `useSubscription().entitled`, is now the gate.** `LiveScreen` and `PicksHomeScreen` were moved; the narrower hook survives only in the Settings card that *describes* the app subscription. Gating on the narrow one would have paywalled every Whop member — charging them twice for what they already bought. Promoted into CLAUDE.md §6 as an invariant.
- **Ladder**: Weekly $9.99 / Monthly $29.99 / Annual $199.99, six-month Season Pass retired (nothing was live, nothing to migrate). Weekly carries **no trial** — a 7-day trial on a 7-day term is a free week that renews into another free week for anyone willing to cancel and resubscribe. Per-month for weekly is 52/12, not four weeks to a month, which would understate the real cost by 8% in the direction that flatters us; `savingsPct` is consequently negative for weekly and the paywall hides the badge rather than inventing a saving.
- **Verified**: `verify_discord_link.ts` (44 assertions, new — including source-level guards that the app never writes either table, that only Discord-verified emails are stored as verified, that the Whop webhook never touches roles, and that the role sync reads only `subscriptions`), `verify_billing.ts` (83, up from 72), `verify_auth.ts` (41), pytest **1303 passed**, and `tsc` **byte-identical to `origin/master` (57 = 57, 0 in touched files)** against a `git worktree --detach origin/master` baseline. One real bug caught while writing the checks: the Whop email backfill used `.ilike`, so an address containing `_` would have attached someone else's membership to the wrong account — changed to `.eq` on the lowered address.
- **Deliberately NOT done**: the migration is not applied and the Discord app/bot/Whop product do not exist yet (Matt's steps, runbook in `mobile/docs/DISCORD_LINKING.md`); no periodic reconciliation sweep of `last_sync_error`, so a Discord outage during a renewal batch leaves those members on their old role until something re-triggers a sync; the gate is still client-side, since `picks` stays anon-readable for the website and the Claude-mobile workflow — `has_app_access()` exists for real enforcement and supersedes `has_active_subscription()`, which cannot see a Whop-paid member.
