**Session summary (2026-08-30, session 144 — CLV across a moved line: the signal line vs the closing line for NCAAF, MLB and WNBA):**
- Matt: *"NCAAF, MLB and WNBA. Can you add CLV for closing rate and show the signal line and the closing line. We do this on NFL so mirror that set up... This is another indicator to show users how our model helps them get the best line."*
- **The blocker was a guard, not a gap.** CLV was already running for MLB (1,323 of 3,231 BETs) and WNBA (461 of 842); NCAAF had none only because 10 of its 11 BETs are live-only. Coverage stalled at ~45% because `_capture_clv` skipped any pick whose line had moved by close — correctly, since Over 44.5 at −110 and Over 46.5 at −110 are different bets and differencing their prices is arithmetic on nothing.
- **The side effect is what Matt was actually asking about.** Because of that guard, `closing_line` was only ever written when it EQUALLED `scored_line` — so the app's `ClvCard` "Line 44.5 → 46.5" row, gated on `scored_line !== closing_line`, was **unreachable code**. The one number a user most wants (what we gave them vs where the market closed) was structurally invisible.
- **The fix, per Matt's call: line CLV alongside price CLV, not instead of it.** NEW `paper_tracker._line_clv_pts` measures how far the NUMBER moved toward the pick side, in points — the generalised form of the NFL card models' `isNflLineOnly` / `computeMovement lineOnly` concept. `clv_pct` keeps its exact meaning and stays same-line-only, so **no published number moves**. NEW columns `picks.line_clv_pts` + `picks.clv_beat_close` (the one verdict: line CLV where the number moved, price CLV where it held) — stored rather than derived so a consumer cannot silently read the wrong one. Migration `add_line_clv_to_picks.sql`, applied to production; 1,795 already-captured picks backfilled (1,465 line CLV — the rest are moneyline, which has no line), 280 beat the close, zero non-zero line CLV as expected since every captured row cleared the same-line guard by construction.
- **The idempotency gate moved from `clv_pct IS NULL` to `clv_captured_at IS NULL`,** because a moved-line pick is now captured legitimately WITHOUT a `clv_pct` and the old gate would have re-processed every one of them on every settle, forever. Verified first: the two columns were 1:1 across all 1,795 captured picks, so the switch is a no-op on existing rows.
- **TWO PRODUCTION BUGS FOUND BY VERIFYING THE CHANGE BEFORE SHIPPING IT, both pre-existing, both fixed here.**
  1. **1,046 of the 1,249 unmeasured MLB+WNBA prop bets were stamped `created_at` AFTER their own `commence_time`.** Their `scored_line` was never a number DK had up pre-game — only **10%** of the MLB ones had it appear anywhere in DK's pre-game history for that market. Recording the close across a moved line without a guard would have manufactured ~1,046 beat-the-close verdicts, **nearly all positive**, because a stale number reads as a favourable move. The first cut of this analysis looked like a +86% beat-the-close rate on MLB props and was chased down rather than published — twice, in fact: an earlier query was missing the `market` filter that `_closing_prop_odds` applies and was comparing a hits line to a total-bases line. NEW guard: `created_at <= commence_time`. With it, **203 of 203** surviving pre-game picks have a signal line DK actually quoted, of which 68 genuinely moved. (§1c: a restore or backfill must preserve `created_at`. These rows are what it looks like when that did not happen — the underlying restamping is NOT fixed here, only excluded from measurement.)
  2. **`_backfill_clv` had never converged, and could not.** Its date scan listed any date holding an un-captured pick, so a date whose picks are ALL permanently unmeasurable sat at the head of the queue forever and the 40 oldest dates were re-walked on every settle instead of advancing. Measured: **eleven of the twelve oldest queued dates had zero capturable picks** — 377 picks re-listed and re-skipped every run. That is why only 1,795 of ~3,400 eligible picks had CLV despite a backfill that had been running for days. The scan now bounds on `created_at <= commence_time`, mirroring the capture guard, so the queue drains.
- **What the change is actually worth, measured rather than assumed: 203 newly capturable settled bets (68 on a genuinely moved line)** — not the ~1,400 the raw un-captured count suggests. The rest of that backlog is bad timestamps, not moved lines. Stated plainly because the tempting version of this entry is the one that claims 1,400.
- **NCAAF needed no code.** `ncaaf_spread` → `spreads` already resolves through `MODELS`, and DK carries NCAAF `h2h` / `spreads` / `totals` (33k / 27k / 26k snapshots since 2026-08-22), so its picks capture CLV the moment they settle. Its one pre-game BET is still unsettled.
- **App (Matt's chosen surface — pick detail only).** `ClvCard` rewritten: gates on `clv_captured_at` (not `clv_pct`, which would hide the card for exactly the picks with the most to show), headline in **points** when the number moved and **pp** when it held, and an explicit **Signal line / Closing line** pair rendered from the pick's own side via `formatSideLine` — so an away spread reads `+5` not the stored `-5`. Aggregate rates (model detail, Discord recap) deliberately left alone: Matt scoped the surface to pick detail, and widening a published rate is a separate call.
- **Verification.** Full suite **1,324 passed / 0 failed** (+25: NEW `tests/test_line_clv.py` with 20 behavioural tests on the sign convention, plus 5 guard tests in `test_clv_props.py`). The sign tests pin line CLV as the exact negation of the app's entry-frame `computeMovement`, so a drift between them can't put a red chip on a pick the CLV card calls a win — and they pin the HOME-relative spread convention that has produced a wrong threshold twice (§4). Mobile `tsc --noEmit` error SET is **byte-identical to `origin/master`** in a detached worktree (28 pre-existing `queries.ts`/`verify_player_log.ts` cast errors, 0 in touched files) — three new fixture errors from the widened `Pick` type were fixed, not suppressed. `get_advisors(security)` after the migration: no new findings (the two columns inherit the `picks` table grants; no new object).

**Session summary (2026-08-30, session 143 — the published stake is the exact number: units go to two decimals):**
- Matt, on a `ncaaf_spread` card (Florida Atlantic +27.5 at -115): *"Can we make this the unit size is exact. It should be 1.15 instead of 1.2."* The card read **1.2u → 1u**. The bet is 1.15 laid to win 1 — 1.2u is a **different bet**, ~4.3% more money on every -115 ticket, and the gap widens on odd prices (-135 published as 1.4u is 3.7% over). Branch `claude/unit-size-precision-67ng3p`.
- **Cause: `fmt_units` / `formatUnits` rounded half-up at ONE decimal.** That was fine while stakes were whole or half units, but the stake became price-aware (risk = conviction / (dec − 1)), and **every negative American price divides out exactly at two decimals** — -115 → 1.15, -105 → 1.05, -118 → 1.18. One decimal was throwing away a digit the number actually has. Now two decimals, trailing zeros trimmed, so "2u", "3.5u" and "1.1u" are unchanged and only the previously-rounded prices move.
- **Both mirrors changed together, as they must** — `tracking/discord_notifier.fmt_units` (Discord channels + recap) and `mobile/src/lib/thresholds.formatUnits` (app). A stake that reads 1.15u in the channel and 1.2u on the card is two different bets for one pick.
- **Two rounding traps, both real, both pinned by tests.** (1) The half-up floor expression stays explicit (`floor(u*100 + 0.5)/100`): Python's `%.2f` is half-to-EVEN and JS `toFixed` is half-up, so the languages disagree on a tie by default. (2) Trimming now **splits on the decimal point** rather than stripping zeros off the whole string — `"20.00".rstrip("0")` is `"2"`, which would have published a 20u exposure total as 2u. Both are commented at the call site and asserted.
- **`-115` added to the parity fixture's ODDS axis** (88 cases, was 80) with a coverage assertion that some case renders `1.15u` — the two-decimal branch is now untestable-to-lose. `tests/test_unit_sizing_parity.py` and `mobile/scripts/verify_units_parity.ts` both read it, so a drift in either language fails.
- **Card layout: the Stake column widened to `flex: 1.4`.** It was an even fifth of a 5-stat row and already wrapped at "1.2u → 1u" on a narrow phone; one more character would have pushed it to three lines. Typography untouched — no font shrinking, no `adjustsFontSizeToFit`, nothing platform-dependent.
- **Verification:** full pytest **1,304 passed / 0 failed** (was 1,300 + 3 pre-existing-expectation failures this change corrected, + 1 new test). `npx tsc --noEmit` error set **byte-identical to `origin/master`** via a `git worktree --detach` baseline, 0 errors in touched files. `verify_units_parity` 88/88 PASS. `verify_units` fails on 24 assertions **identically to master** (its conviction-tier expectations are stale since the 1u-3u scale was retired) — diffed as an error SET, not a count; every formatting assertion in it passes.
- **Three Discord test expectations were UPDATED, not loosened**: -154 → `1.54u` (was 1.5u), -118 → `1.18u` (was 1.2u), -135/-132 → `1.35u`/`1.32u`. Each was asserting the old rounding, so each is exactly the failure this change is supposed to produce.
- **Not a model update** under §1b — nothing about what a model does or whether it fires changed, only how the stake is rendered — so no `Updated-By` trailer. Thresholds, Kelly sizing and the BET decision are untouched; `unitsFor` / `units_for` return unrounded floats as before, so exposure sums and settled P&L are bit-identical.
- **Flagged, NOT fixed (pre-existing):** `mobile/scripts/verify_units.ts` carries 24 stale conviction-tier assertions from before the 1u–3u scale was retired, and fails identically on master. It is not in the pytest gate, so it fails silently; worth a pass to retarget it at flat conviction, but out of scope here.


**Session summary (2026-08-30, session 142 — settled LIVE bets were invisible in the Record tab; and #307 let the session-114 repair rows into both recaps):**
- Matt: "The record tab is not showing the live bets that settled for NCAAF, not sure if that's a similar issue across other sports." **Not NCAAF-specific — every live bet ever settled was invisible in the Record tab**: all four track-record views carry `p.is_live IS NOT TRUE`, so 92 settled live BETs (83 MLB + 9 NCAAF) never reached it. NCAAF was simply where the gap showed, because live is all it has settled so far (`ncaaf_spread` shows 0 picks), so the sport read as completely empty. Branch `claude/record-tab-live-bets-ncaaf-jlnk45`, PR #302.
- **The exclusion was not a mistake, and this is the trap: `picks.is_live` carries TWO populations.** (1) real in-play picks from the live scorers — 325 rows across `mlb_live_*` / `ncaaf_live_*`; (2) the **session-114 repair rows — 14,113 PRE-GAME prop picks** retroactively flagged `is_live` because they were scored against in-play prices after first pitch. A naive "drop the filter" resurrects contaminated bets into the honest record. So the filter could only be *narrowed*, never removed. `model_id LIKE '%\_live\_%'` separates them exactly, verified against every distinct model_id in `picks`: all 5 live models match, none of the 17 repaired prop models do, and **zero non-`is_live` models match**, so the clause is purely additive on `is_live` rows.
- **Decision (asked): fold live into the sport totals** — Matt chose it over a separate "Live" line or leaving Record pre-game only. A live bet is now just a bet, per §1c.
- **Migration `track_record_include_live_models` (applied)** patches `v_public_track_record` + `_daily` via `pg_get_viewdef` + replace (the runline sign-fix technique), and is **idempotent** — it checks for the pattern marker and skips rather than double-wrapping (re-ran it live; numbers unchanged). Live models land in each view's `other` CTE (outside the MLB/WNBA full-outcome whitelist), which is where it takes effect; the occurrences in `clv` / `dates_fo` / `fo_base` are no-ops kept identical so there is one predicate to reason about. `v_model_full_outcome_record` / `_picks` and `mv_scored_pick_outcomes` are deliberately **untouched** (their whitelist already excludes live models). **Measured: 513 → 547 picks, 295-195-23 → 314-210-23, +$0.71 on +$3,400 staked (ROI 10.79% → 10.11%); NCAAF 0 → 9, MLB 425 → 450.** `_daily` still reconciles to the penny.
- **Then master moved 13 commits under the branch, and #307 ("In-play picks count in the daily record") had done the recap half — with two defects this merge fixes.** Worth recording because both are the kind that pass every test they ship with:
  1. **Its mobile half was inert.** `computeDailyResults` was taught to count live picks, but `fetchDayPicks` still filtered `.not('is_live','is',true)` server-side — the client counted rows the query never returned. This branch had already removed that filter (safe: the busiest day on record is ~3.2k rows *including* every `is_live` row against the 5k cap, and NONE rows sort last).
  2. **Its Discord half over-included.** `_SETTLED_SQL` dropped the `is_live` clause entirely, so the recap counted **65 settled session-114 repair rows (20-45, −$1,493.03)** as real bets — fabricated losses that session 114 had removed from every record.
- **The design disagreement, resolved as complementary rather than either/or.** #307's commit says it moved from a model_id regex to `pick.is_live` because "the row already carries is_live. Two sources that can disagree." That is right for *"is this pick in-play?"* — `is_live` is the authority, and it drives the split display and the CLV exclusion. It is insufficient for *"is that flag telling the truth?"*, where 14,113 rows carry it wrongly by construction. Both checks now sit side by side in `computeDailyResults` and in `_SETTLED_SQL`, each commented with which question it answers.
- **Merge resolution kept master's structure throughout** (pre-game/in-play split line, explicit CLV exclusion, retired-model handling, de-positionalised fixtures) and added the contamination guard on top. Master's `test_the_recap_query_no_longer_excludes_live` asserted the substring `"p.is_live IS NOT TRUE" not in _SETTLED_SQL`, which the correct narrowed predicate legitimately contains inside its OR — retargeted at the **bare** exclusion line so the tripwire still catches the wrong clause without failing on the right one.
- **NEW `thresholds.isLiveModel` / `isContaminatedPregamePick`** are the app-side mirror of the SQL predicate, so the phone and the DB cannot disagree about what counts. `_SETTLED_SQL` became a **raw** string: `'%%\_live\_%%'` in a normal string is an invalid Python escape and would warn on Matt's 3.14 (it compiles clean on the sandbox's 3.11, so it would only have shown up on his machine).
- **Verification:** full pytest **1,206 passed / 0 failed** post-merge. Every new test mutation-tested: reverting the SQL fails exactly the 2 new Python tests, reverting the client guard fails 7 daily-recap checks. `npx tsc --noEmit` error set **byte-identical to master**. `verify_daily_results` and `verify_retired_models` ALL PASS; `verify_units` fails identically on master (pre-existing stale fixtures).
- **Flagged, NOT fixed (pre-existing, unreachable on the happy path):** `computeBuiltInModelStats` reads `fetchSettledPicks`, whose `SETTLED_PICK_COLUMNS` omits `is_live`, so if the `v_model_full_outcome_record` fetch ever FAILS the Models tab's fallback would count repair rows for MLB/WNBA props. Fixing it needs an extra column plus a settled-pick cache-version bump; the view path takes precedence normally.

**Session summary (2026-08-30, session 141 — the monitor becomes a one-stop operational dashboard: Models + Ops views):**
- Matt: "add a section for active models, number of discord users and subscribers. picks per model over time and high level performance metrics for the model I want this to be a onestop shop dash for all things operational / monitoring / performance." The Live view (session 138) answered "is the pipeline running"; this answers "and is it any good, and who is it reaching". One page, three views — `Live | Models | Ops`.
- **Models view.** *Performance*: settled BET record per model (W-L-P, units, ROI, last settled date). *Roster*: every `model_action_thresholds` row — the authority on whether a model FIRES, since it is what the app's action filter reads and what the daily sync writes — LEFT JOIN LATERAL'd to its active `model_registry` artifact, so **registered-but-untrained** (NHL totals/puckline, the golf models) is a visible state rather than something discovered at score time; header counts live / paused / no-artifact. *Picks per model over time*: 14 days from `picks`, per-model sparklines plus a slate total, live picks excluded (they churn every pass and would swamp the pre-game board beside them).
- **Two record conventions carried over deliberately, both pinned in `docs/monitoring.md`.** (1) Performance reads **`mv_scored_pick_outcomes`, never `v_model_full_outcome_record`** — measured: the view is 1,596ms / 568k buffer hits because it re-grades every pick against current thresholds, which cannot sit behind a poll loop at any rate; the matview is the same grading, materialised, at 285ms, refreshed by `--step refresh-outcomes` after settle. (2) **ROI is over the PRICED subset with the count shown beside it** — a prob-only pick with no DK price has NULL `profit_units`, so `mlb_prop_batter_hr` renders "20 of 252 priced" in amber rather than letting a 42-210 record carry a +12.9% ROI computed from a twentieth of it. Fabricating -110 for the rest is exactly how a record-only model grows invented P&L.
- **Ops view.** Subscribers (`subscriptions`), Discord members/online + delivered posts, push devices, linked sportsbooks, open feedback threads — plus pipeline runs and health in the same place. **Two tiles read 0 by design and say so:** subscribers (billing ships dark, `BILLING_ENABLED` defaults false) and push devices (the native push build was never made). Both light up on their own when those turn on; neither is a fault to chase.
- **Discord member counts are structurally impossible over a webhook, and the module says so instead of faking it.** An incoming webhook is a write-only URL — it can post to a channel, it cannot see who is in the server. The only endpoint that returns a count is `GET /guilds/{id}?with_counts=true`, which needs a **bot token**. `monitoring/discord_stats.py` is inert until `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` + `DISCORD_GUILD_ID` are set (no privileged intents, no channel permissions — the bot only has to be a member) and reports 401 / 403 / 404 / 429 **distinctly**, because "no number" has four different fixes. Setup runbook in `docs/monitoring.md`.
- **NEW `monitoring/cache.py` — a module-level TTL cache, and the choice of *module-level* is the point.** The live panels tail an index and are cheap; these are not. Caching here makes cost independent of BOTH **viewer count** (five people share one entry) and **poll rate** (the meta tick stays at 10s while a 300s panel hits the database twelve times an hour) — the same Disk IO shape that cost a whole session in #291. On a refresh failure the **stale value is served**, since a five-minute-old ROI beats an empty panel because the pooler dropped a connection; every cached panel ships its own `age_s` and the UI renders it, so an old number states its age rather than implying "now". TTLs: roster 60 / series 120 / performance 300 / community 300 / Discord 600.
- **Every new query was cost-measured against production before it shipped** (the session-138 rule — a seq scan is multiplied by the poll rate): roster 0.6ms, performance 285ms, series ~60ms. `picks_over_time` is bounded on the indexed `game_date`, the same lesson `pick_counts` taught the hard way.
- **Three defects found only by rendering the views in Chromium**, none of which review caught: (1) **a handler collision that broke the whole view switcher** — the time-window handler selected `.seg button`, which also matched the new view buttons and overwrote their onclick, so clicking "Models" reconnected the stream with `window=NaN` and never switched views (diagnosed by same-tick DOM evaluation: `aria-pressed` updated, `.hidden` did not; fixed by giving the window group an id and scoping both handlers); (2) a **dual-magnitude chart** — signals are ~3% of scored, so the blue bar was 2px inside a grey slab, fixed by charting one measure; (3) the partial-priced ROI reading as a full record, fixed with the amber "N of M priced" flag above.
- **Verification.** 20 new tests (56 in `tests/test_monitoring.py`). Full suite **1,226 passed / 0 failed**; the `origin/master` baseline in a detached worktree is **1,208 passed / 0 failed** — +18 tests, zero regressions. All four new store queries run against **production Postgres** via the Supabase MCP. Both new views rendered and inspected in Chromium with no console errors (Models: 10 performance rows, 11 roster rows "7 live / 2 paused / 2 no artifact", 5 sparklines; Ops: community tiles, the Discord setup guidance in its unconfigured state, 2 pipeline runs, 6 health checks).
- **Matt, to light up the two dark tiles (optional, neither blocks anything):** create a Discord bot and set `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` + `DISCORD_GUILD_ID` on the Railway worker (steps in `docs/monitoring.md`); subscribers fills itself when billing is enabled.

**Session summary (2026-08-30, session 140 — CLAUDE.md split: 909 KB → 31.7 KB, the session log archived, the threshold SQL generated):**
- Mike: "what do we do about the claude.md char limit" → then "do it all". Measured first: **909 KB / 5,393 lines ≈ 225k tokens re-read at the start of every session**, and **76% of it (680 KB, 194 blocks) was the session log** — an append-only changelog duplicating git history. Growth rate over the preceding 8 commits was ~2.7 KB per commit, so this was compounding.
- **The cause was a rule, not neglect.** The file header said *"Update this file after every commit"*, and 192 sessions obeyed it. Fixing the size without fixing the rule would have rebuilt it inside a quarter — so the durable change is the new standing rule in §1b: session summaries go to `docs/sessions/<YYYY-MM>.md`, subsystem reference goes to its own `docs/` file, and **only a rule that governs FUTURE work gets promoted into CLAUDE.md**.
- **The log is archived, not deleted.** 192 entries → `docs/sessions/2026-{03..08}.md`, newest-first within each month, plus `docs/sessions/README.md` — a 192-row index (date · file · title) so a future session greps for "session 106" instead of loading a 288 KB month. Two truncated empty stubs (`**Session summary (2026-`) were dropped as artifacts; **every other block was verified byte-present in the archive (192/192)**.
- **Reference sections split into `docs/`:** per-sport ops → `docs/sports/{mlb,wnba,nba,nhl,ufc,golf,nfl,ncaaf}.md`; plus `thresholds.md`, `mobile_picks_prompt.md`, `discord.md`, `live_betting.md`, `opening_signals.md`, `health_checks.md`, `signal_timing.md`, `config_topology.md`, `testing.md`, and `docs/history/` for provenance (build state, learnings, the April-era "next steps" that were stale).
- **The three duplicated threshold SQL blocks are now GENERATED — and they had already drifted.** `scripts/emit_threshold_sql.py` reads `ACTION_THRESHOLDS` + `PAUSED_MODELS` (emitted as comments) + `PROB_ONLY_MODELS` (edge clause omitted) + `MODEL_MIN_ODDS` (price floor) straight from `config.py`. The hand-maintained copies had drifted: measured against the block actually pasted into Claude mobile, **3 models were missing** (`nba_over_under`, `nba_spread`, `nfl_prop_market`) and **4 were stale** (paused models still listed, so the phone would surface picks the scorer has stopped making). An earlier draft of this entry said "~50 of 70, every NFL prop missing" — that compared the block's line count against `ACTION_THRESHOLDS` including its 26 paused entries, and overstated it. `--prefix p.` emits the joined-query variant; live models are excluded by default (`--live` to include) so the pre-game board behaviour is unchanged.
- **What stayed in CLAUDE.md (31.7 KB):** §0 reply format, §1/§1b/§1c (verbatim — the standing rules and the pick rule are the point of the file), §2 purpose, §3 business logic, §4 conventions, §5 commands, §6 config topology (condensed; full text in `docs/config_topology.md`), §7 **hard-won lessons**, §8 a 30-second state summary, §9 the docs map.
- **§7 is the part that took judgement.** The session log is not pure noise — it is where the traps live. Before archiving, the recurring ones were extracted and restated as rules in their own right: THE EVALUATION RULE (grade `BET`+`AVOID`+`NONE`, with the three coverage traps and the clean windows), validate grading before moving a cut, require a plateau not a peak, time-split every situational finding, the "latest snapshot" leak, TEXT timestamps needing a parse before comparison, ET-not-UTC, a sparse column deleting the training matrix, **commit the `.pkl`**, a model in `MODELS` without a `FEATURE_MAP` entry killing scoring for every sport, an empty board vs a broken pipeline, health checks that gate on the thing that breaks, a swallowed exception in an expected-empty channel, the Supabase anon-grant trap, the Odds API quota header — plus the verification standards (`git worktree` not `git stash` for a baseline; error-SET diffs not counts; check whether deps install before hand-waving).
- **New conventions promoted into §4** because each has caused a real bug: season labels differ by sport (ending-year for NHL/NBA/NCAAF), team ids are 3-letter except NCAAF, and **`scored_line` is always the HOME number** — the away-cover sign that produced a wrong threshold twice.
- **Cross-references repointed, including in code.** 14 docs and 10 source files had ``docs/mobile_picks_prompt.md``/``docs/thresholds.md``/``docs/sports/nfl.md``-style pointers into a file that no longer has those sections; all now name the doc. `docs/sessions/` is deliberately left verbatim — it is historical record. Two docs with their own internal numbering (`nfl_props_model.md`, `ncaaf_model_plan.md`) were skipped for the colliding numbers.
- **Two collisions caught during the split, both would have destroyed existing content:** `docs/monitoring.md` already existed (6.5 KB runbook) and was overwritten by `docs/monitoring.md` — restored from `origin/master` and `docs/monitoring.md` appended as a "Design notes and invariants" section instead. `docs/push_notifications.md` already existed, so `docs/push_notifications.md` was appended to it and the redundant `push_setup.md` removed. The old §7 first-time-setup command block was preserved by appending it to `docs/local_ops.md`.
- **NEW `tests/test_claude_md_size.py`** — the tripwire that stops the regrowth: CLAUDE.md ≤ 45 KB, **no `**Session summary` block may appear in it**, every `docs/…md` the §9 index names must exist, and every month archive must be listed in the sessions README. Proven to detect the bug it exists for (appending one fake session summary fails the test; removing it passes).
- **Verification:** a completeness check over every substantive line of the original (4,120 lines after excluding the now-generated SQL) found **30 absent, all of them intentional** — the old header, the deliberately edited/renamed lines, and section headings that became doc titles. Full suite **14 failed / 1,221 passed**, and the failure SET is a strict subset of `origin/master`'s (15 failed / 1,216 passed, measured in a `git worktree` — the extra one is the documented worktree-path assertion in `test_nfl_opener`). So: zero regressions, +4 new passing tests, +1 test that master fails only because of the worktree.
- **The archive's own section references were repointed too** (Mike's call, same session). This was left verbatim at first on the principle that a changelog is a record — but a record full of pointers into sections that no longer exist is a worse record. Only the unambiguous numbers were rewritten (§11, §13, §16-§33). Two classes were deliberately left as written and explained in a header note on every month file: **session numbers written as §** (§113, §118, §126b/c, §131 — a blanket rewrite would have mangled these, and §11 must not match inside §113), and **low-numbered refs (§0-§9), whose meaning depends on the entry's age** — in a pre-split entry §4/§6/§7 mean build state / model registry / commands, in a post-split one they mean conventions / config topology / hard-won lessons. Guessing per-occurrence would put words in a past session's mouth.
- **One regex trap worth remembering:** the lookahead that stops `§11` matching inside `§113` also stops `§22.` at the end of a sentence. Sub-numbers and sentence periods need different treatment — `(?![0-9])(?!\.\d)`, not `(?![0-9.])`.
- **Net:** CLAUDE.md 908,729 → 31,663 bytes (**96.5% smaller**, ~7.9k tokens instead of ~225k), with nothing deleted — everything is in `docs/` and in git history.

**Session summary (2026-08-30, session 135 — the two binary MLB live models RETIRED (registry, artifacts, thresholds), totals stays):**
- Matt: "Remove the failing live betting models for MLB." That is `mlb_live_win_prob` and `mlb_live_runline`, paused the previous day (session 133/`docs/live_betting.md`) after the settled-live sweep; `mlb_live_total_runs` is the profitable one and is untouched. Branch `claude/remove-mlb-live-betting-ldnn98`.
- **Why removal rather than a longer pause, stated plainly:** a pause exists so a forward record can earn an unpause. Neither of these can earn one. They lose at EVERY cut (win_prob 15 bets 6-9 −34.1%, runline 14 bets 5-9 −39.9%) and get WORSE as the probability floor rises — average model probability 0.73–0.76 against a 36–40% realised win rate. That is overconfidence, i.e. a CALIBRATION failure, and more NONE rows at the same overconfidence teach nothing a threshold could act on. Their 5.3% / 5.9% holdout CalErr — both above the 5% go-live gate — said so before either model took a bet.
- **Removed from `config.LIVE_MODELS`, which is what actually stops them.** That registry drives the live scorer, the trainer (`--all-live`) and `LIVE_FEATURE_MAP`, so a retired model cannot score, be retrained, or write a row of any kind. Also removed from `PAUSED_MODELS` (nothing left to pause) and all three threshold dicts; `LIVE_FEATURE_MAP` entries and their `compute_live_target` labels are gone (`LIVE_PREGAME_H2H_CONTEXT` is kept but wired to nothing — documented as the starting layout for any revived WP model). `models/saved/mlb_live_{win_prob,runline}_*.pkl` deleted; both `model_registry` rows set `is_active = 0` and both `model_action_thresholds` rows deleted (applied directly — the daily `threshold_sync` prunes them from master anyway once this merges).
- **The one thing retirement must NOT break, and the load-bearing part of this change: their existing picks keep grading.** 4 unsettled BETs and 29 settled ones sit in `picks`, and a pick that existed is the bet of record (§1c). `_market_for_pick` resolves live ids THROUGH `LIVE_MODELS`, so deleting the entries would have dropped both to the `'h2h'` default — and a runline pick graded as a moneyline turns a failed −1.5 cover into a win. New `paper_tracker._RETIRED_MODEL_MARKETS` (win_prob → h2h, runline → spreads) is consulted first and keeps that math right forever.
- **Mobile: NEW `thresholds.RETIRED_MODELS` + `isModelRetired()`, deliberately NOT a reuse of PAUSED_MODELS.** `passesActionFilter` prefers the SERVER threshold row, and that row outlives the model until the next sync prune — while it does, it reports `paused=false`. Dropping the ids from the bundled `PAUSED_MODELS` list without a retired guard would therefore have made an old live BET read as actionable again. Retired models are also hidden from the Models tab (listing a model that can never pick again is a lie). `MODEL_META` labels and the `gameMarketForModel` mapping are KEPT so the picks they already made still render with a name and still drive the Line Movement card.
- **Verification:** full pytest suite **1,090 passed / 1 skipped, 0 failed** (baseline on an `origin/master` worktree: 1,088 passed + 1 failure that is purely a worktree-path assertion in `test_nfl_opener`, so zero regressions and +2 net tests). Three existing live tests used the retired ids as generic fixtures and were re-pointed at real state rather than deleted: the two-lane lock tests now monkeypatch a synthetic two-lane registry (per-lane independence is registry-agnostic, and MLB no longer HAS two live lanes), plus a new single-lane test against the real registry. New tests pin the whole retirement contract — absent from every registry and threshold dict, no training label, and `_market_for_pick` still returning h2h/spreads. Mobile: NEW `scripts/verify_retired_models.ts` **12/12**, including the stale-server-row case (temporarily removing the guard fails exactly 2 checks, so the script detects the bug it exists for); `npx tsc --noEmit` = **28 errors, error set byte-identical to master** (stash-compared), 0 in touched files; verify sweep — ncaaf_ui / signal_counts / live_tracked / preferred_book (54) / custom_model_filters / sharp_score / betslip / daily_results all pass.
- **Reviving one is a retrain, not a config edit** — artifacts are deleted and the registry rows deactivated, and it should clear the 5% calibration gate first.

**Session summary (2026-08-30, session 137 — every signal and live bet now carries the time it posted):**
- Matt: "Add the time the signal bet or live bet posted to the app." Mobile-only; no DB/pipeline/scorer/threshold/model changes, no new deps. Branch `claude/signal-live-bet-post-time-7qmo6h`.
- **`created_at` IS the post time — but only for a LOCKED row, and that is the whole design of the helper.** A locked row is never rewritten (game picks at the first scoring run, props at the first signal on a confirmed lineup, live bets at the first BET in the lane, NFL cards insert-once), so its `created_at` is the instant the bet was given. For anything that re-scores, `created_at` is the LATEST pass and stamping it would misreport when the bet posted — so NEW `pickTimingInfo(pick)` (markets.ts) refuses two classes: non-BET rows (AVOID, and the NCAAF "watching" NONE rows that delete+rescore every pass) and unlocked look-ahead previews.
- The preview guard is a **seam that is a provable no-op today** — `UNLOCKED_LOOKAHEAD_SPORTS` was emptied 2026-08-28 — and is kept because a sport re-added there would re-price after posting. The verify script pins BOTH sides by adding UFC to the set, asserting the stamp disappears, then restoring it; an always-false branch tested only in its false state proves nothing.
- **One chip replaces two.** The card previously carried a live-lock chip ("Locked inning 3 — bet of record") with no time and an NFL chip showing a day OR a time; now a single timing chip covers every sport: `Posted 11:07 AM ET`, `Locked 8:42 PM ET · inning 3 — bet of record` (`· Q4` for NCAAF), `Locked Tue 8/18 · 9:31 AM ET` (NFL opener) / `Priced Sun 8:05 AM ET` (wind). Exempt from the 2-chip hero cap, like injury — a live number is minutes old and an NFL opener is days old, so the timestamp is not decoration.
- **NFL gained the time it was missing.** `nflTimingInfo` is untouched (its verbs/notes are what `verify_nfl_movement` pins) and `pickTimingInfo` delegates to it, but the card label previously showed `gameDayLabelET(created_at) ?? formatGameTimeET(...)` — day OR time, so an opener locked days out showed "Locked Tue 8/18" and never said when.
- NEW `formatStampET` (format.ts): time alone when the stamp is today in ET, `day · time` otherwise. Joined with a separator rather than a comma because the ET day label already carries its own ("Wed, 8/26") — caught by the verify script, not by review.
- `NflTimingCard` → **`PickTimingCard`** (old file deleted), rendered on PickDetail for every sport instead of `pick.sport === 'NFL'`: full day+time headline plus a note explaining what the lock means for that pick class (live: "not the current" price; props: lineup lock; game: first run of the day).
- **Verification:** NEW `scripts/verify_pick_timing.ts` **24/24** (stamp formatting incl. the off-day case and a missing timestamp; game vs prop notes; live label with/without a period and the NCAAF quarter; NFL opener vs wind verbs; AVOID, NONE, blank `created_at` and the preview seam all refusing). Two real defects surfaced by writing it: the double comma above, and my own use of a UTC date as "today" (`toISOString().slice(0,10)` is tomorrow after 8pm ET — the exact class of bug `todayET()` exists for). `npx tsc --noEmit` = **28 errors, error set byte-identical to master** (stash-compared incl. untracked files; the documented `queries.ts` casts + the pre-existing `verify_player_log` fixture), 0 in touched files. All touched files parse through the real `babel-preset-expo`. verify_nfl_movement / signal_counts / preferred_book (54) / line_shop / betslip / live_tracked / custom_model_filters all pass.
- **Follow-up shipped in the same PR** (Matt: "Yes add to discord as well. It should be the time it writes to the database to know the first minute we get it") — see the Discord half below.
- JS-only, and touches neither `mobile/package.json` nor `mobile/app.json`, so `mobile-ota.yml`'s native-config guard clears and this reaches installed builds automatically on merge.

- **Discord half (same session, same PR):** every signal post, and the free pick of the day, now carry `posted <time ET>`; the live post's existing stamp was relabelled `priced` → `posted` (same column, same instant — one word across the app and the channel).
- **The column is the requirement, and production proved it.** `opening_signals.locked_at` was already in the query, and using it would have been a one-word change — but it is the CAPTURE step's clock, not the pick's. Measured over August (150 locked signals): capture lags the pick's own write by **28 minutes on average, up to 382.9 minutes (6.4 hours)**, with 14 signals more than 5 minutes late — including the three 2026-08-29 F5 picks that wrote at 3:18pm ET and were captured at 4:31pm (73.1 min). Stamping locked_at would make a stale signal look newly posted, which is the opposite of what Matt asked for. So both signal producers and the free-pick query now read `picks.created_at` through a `LEFT JOIN LATERAL` (replacing the scalar `dk_bet_link` subquery, so it is one lookup rather than two). **No fallback to locked_at on purpose:** a missing pick row (1 of 150 in August) publishes no stamp rather than a wrong one.
- **Validated against production, not just fakes:** the rewritten query runs on Supabase and returns the expected shape, and `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` is **2.3 ms** — index-driven (`idx_picks_date` + `idx_picks_model`), no regression on the scalar subquery it replaced.
- **Precision is per surface:** seconds in-play (a live total moves a full run on one scoring play, so its age matters to the second), minutes pre-game (a stable price makes second-level precision false precision), and the ET **date** is prefixed when it isn't today's — an NFL opener posts days before kickoff, and a bare "9:31 AM ET" on a Saturday board reads as this morning. The date joins with a comma, not the middle dot the embed field uses as its own separator.
- **Verification:** `tests/test_discord_notifier.py` +9 (real producer → real renderer, the pattern that caught the live `KeyError`; minute-vs-second precision; the day prefix; no pick row → no stamp; a **source tripwire on both producers** that the stamp comes from `p.created_at`/`pk.created_at`; the free-pick embed; the IP-leak guard re-run with the new field present; a malformed timestamp degrading rather than raising) and `tests/test_discord_live_field.py` updated for the rename. Discord suites **102 passed**; full suite **1,152 passed / 1 skipped / 0 failed** against a master worktree baseline of **1,142 passed + 1 failed** (that failure is the documented worktree-path assertion in `test_nfl_opener`) — +10 tests, zero regressions.

**Session summary (2026-08-30, session 139 — an evening NCAAF pick can finally settle: the ET/UTC duplicate games row):**
- Matt: "The first day the live over under went 2-5. Can you check to see if there are any concerns or if it's because first week" → then "Yes fix the settlement issue." Branch `claude/live-over-under-performance-led42i`.
- **The 2-5 was really 3-5, and the missing win is the bug.** `ncaaf_live_total` settled 2-5 on 8/29 with one pick left open — **Memphis @ UNLV Over 36.5, final 48, a WIN** — stuck because the pick sits on `NCAAF_2026-08-29_memphis_unlv` (no score) while CFBD wrote the final to `NCAAF_2026-08-30_memphis_unlv`. The odds ingestor dates a game by its EASTERN kickoff; `parse_games` uses CFBD's UTC `start_date`, so a 10:19pm ET kick exists twice. Flagged in session 132, unfixed until now.
- **It was never a live-totals problem — it was about to break every NCAAF model.** Surveying the whole table: **15 duplicated matchups**, all 2026, all late kickoffs, and the split is perfectly consistent — picks land on the `live` row (9 of the 15 already carry 6 picks each), scores land on the `cfbd` row. The 9/4 and 9/5 slates were already stacked with picks that could never have graded. Secondary cost: `ingest_ncaaf_results_for_date` selects seasons with any NULL-score game, so the orphan row kept the season permanently "pending" and re-pulled every full-season schedule daily.
- **Fix: mirror the final onto every row that is the same game** (`mirror_scores_to_alias_rows` + the pure `alias_score_updates`), wired into BOTH paths that write finals — the pre-settle results step and the weekly season ingest. Matched on the slug pair within **±1 day**, orientation-corrected. This is the UFC `_resolve_game_rows` precedent, and it beats a settle-time-only fallback because the score lands on the row the picks actually reference — so the app board, `mv_scored_pick_outcomes` and the record views all come right too, not just settlement.
- **Deliberately NOT a re-key of `game_id`** (session 132's reasoning re-confirmed): it is the FK for `ncaaf_team_game_log` and `ncaaf_qb_game` across 2015-2025, so re-deriving the date would orphan a decade of training rows mid-season for zero modelling benefit. The historical data is internally consistent — the mismatch exists only at the boundary with the live odds ingestor.
- **Three guards, each with a test that fails without it:** ±1 day (an annual rivalry repeats the slug pair every season — a naive pair match would have mirrored Michigan-Ohio State 2025 onto 2026, and the first survey query I wrote made exactly that mistake); never overwrite an existing final (a mirrored score is an inference and must not clobber a real result — the guard is in the UPDATE statement as well as the planner, so a concurrent real final wins); and a candidate matched by two conflicting finals is left unscored rather than guessed.
- **Repair applied:** filled 21-27 onto the orphan UNLV row — exactly what the fixed code computes, verified by running the pure function against the real production rows first. Deliberately filled the SCORE only and left grading to the normal settle path rather than hand-stamping a result; `_compute_result` replays it as **WIN +$83.33**, and it grades on the next settle run (14-day window). The other 14 duplicates self-heal on the next `ncaaf-results` run after merge.
- **Verification:** NEW `tests/test_ncaaf_alias_scores.py` **16/16**, incl. a replay of the exact production UNLV rows, accent/punctuation names through the slug (San José State, Hawai'i — both in week-1 picks), swapped orientation, ties, and the writer against a recording fake. **Mutation-tested all three guards**: widening the window fails 2 tests, removing the overwrite guard fails 1, ignoring orientation fails 1 — so the tests detect the bugs they exist for. Full suite **1,185 passed / 0 failed** against a clean-worktree baseline of **1,168 passed + 1 failed** (the documented worktree-only path assertion in `test_nfl_opener`): +16 mine, +1 that only fails in a worktree, **zero regressions**. `_market_for_pick('ncaaf_live_total')` confirmed → `totals` (a fallback to `h2h` would have graded a totals pick as a moneyline).
- **On the 2-5 itself: variance, not a broken model** — at the picks' own average probability (0.66) a day that bad is ~10% likely, and the model passed its 2025 Brier/coverage gates. But the day is not a clean test: the publish-clock freshness guard (#282) merged **mid-slate**, after 7 of the 8 picks, so every loss was made by code that could bet a frozen line. Two watch items, neither actionable at n=8: the three fastest locks (3.7-6.5 min after kickoff, before real game state exists) went 0-3, and 7 of 8 picks were overs on a mismatch-heavy opening week. **No threshold changes** — re-cutting on 8 picks is the exact noise-fitting `docs/thresholds.md` warns about.

**Session summary (2026-08-30, session 138 — Supabase "Disk IO Budget depleting" alert: five missing indexes found and fixed in production):**
- Matt forwarded the Supabase email ("your project is depleting its Disk IO Budget"). Diagnosis via pg_stat_statements: **not data growth — five hot queries were full-table-scanning on every call** because no index covered them. Cumulative disk reads at diagnosis: `scorer._lookup_player_id` (`LOWER(player_name)` on `player_game_log`, 68k calls) **638 GB**; `scorer._book_opener` + the latest-odds lookup family on `odds` (filter `game_id, market, bookmaker` + ORDER BY `snapshot_at` — the planner walked `idx_odds_date` across the whole 1.5M-row table, **2.1 s/call**) **425 GB combined**; `MAX(snapshot_at)` freshness probes full-scanning the 1.5 GB `player_prop_odds` (**7.2 s/call**) **131 GB**; the live loops' `_locked_live_lanes` seq-scanning `picks` every 5s pass (no `game_id` index — **1.78B tuples** read via seq scans); latest-live-state reads on `live_game_state.snapshot_at`. The 5s live cadence (sessions 133/134) multiplied the per-call cost of the first, second and fourth into the budget alert. Branch `claude/database-issue-azfxi1`.
- **Fix: five indexes, applied to production (migration `add_disk_io_indexes`) and mirrored** in `db_setup.py` SQLite SCHEMA_SQL + `supabase_schema.sql` + `data/migrations/add_disk_io_indexes.sql`: `idx_player_game_log_name (lower(player_name), player_type)` — expression index; `idx_odds_book_snap (game_id, market, bookmaker, snapshot_at)` — serves both the ASC opener lookup and the DESC latest lookup; `idx_prop_odds_snapshot (snapshot_at)`; `idx_picks_game (game_id)`; `idx_live_state_snapshot (snapshot_at)`. **Measured after (EXPLAIN ANALYZE, production): name lookup 96 ms → 2.4 ms; book opener 2,111 ms → 1.1 ms** (~90 MB of reads per call → 5 buffers). No scoring/settlement code changed — the queries are unchanged, only indexed. `idx_odds_game` deliberately kept (other readers may use its snapshot_type column; dropping it is a separate decision).
- **Ops lessons for future DDL against this DB via the Supabase MCP** (each learned the hard way this session): (1) the MCP client times out at 60 s but the STATEMENT KEEPS RUNNING server-side — poll `pg_indexes` / `pg_stat_activity` instead of re-firing; (2) a cancelled `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` leaves an INVALID index that silently blocks `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` under the same name — check `pg_index.indisvalid`, and remove it with **`DROP INDEX CONCURRENTLY`** (plain `DROP INDEX` queues for an ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock behind the scorer's minutes-long transactions and, while queued, blocks every reader/writer behind it — it stalled a live-loop DELETE for over a minute before being killed via `pg_terminate_backend`); (3) plain `CREATE INDEX` builds are the right tool here at quiet hours (mid-morning, before the slate) — they block writes only briefly and can't leave invalid leftovers; (4) `pg_cron` is not installed and enabling it was not possible from this session.
- **Verification:** all 5 indexes `indisvalid=true` in production; EXPLAIN ANALYZE before/after on the two worst queries (above); SQLite SCHEMA_SQL builds twice (idempotent) with all 5 new indexes present and the expression index actually chosen by SQLite's planner for the name lookup; performance advisor shows no new findings from the DDL. **The proof is the Disk IO chart** (Supabase → Reports → Database) over the next 24-48h — the daily consumption should collapse, since ~95% of measured disk reads came from the five fixed queries. pg_stat_statements counters are cumulative since long before the fix — compare per-day, not totals.

**Session summary (2026-08-30, session 138 — real-time monitoring dashboard: every API call and every pick, live):**
- Matt: "can we build an application that shows real time flow of api calls to all the apis we are connected to and picks being generated etc. I want to build a high speed, fancy monitoring dashboard." Decisions (asked): **both** a worker-hosted stream and a local viewer, from one codebase; **everything** recorded via one global hook rather than paid APIs only. Branch `claude/api-monitoring-dashboard-ge15r2`. New **`docs/monitoring.md`**; runbook `docs/monitoring.md`.
- **The design question that shaped everything: the scheduler SHELLS OUT.** Every pass is `python run_pipeline.py --step X`, the live loops are separate processes, the NFL card runs with `cwd=nfl/`. So an in-process event bus in the scheduler would have seen almost nothing. The database is the only rendezvous — which also means the local viewer shows the worker's traffic, and history survives a Railway redeploy (its disk does not). Probe flush 0.75s + stream poll 1s ≈ 1–2s end-to-end.
- **One patch instead of 75 call-site edits.** There are ~75 `requests.get` sites across 34 modules and no shared HTTP helper. Wrapping `requests.sessions.Session.request` once covers all of them AND the libraries we don't own (statsapi, nba_api, pybaseball, cloudscraper). Future ingestors are covered with no code change; a host nobody has named shows up as itself rather than vanishing.
- **Two real leaks found by testing, not by reading.** (1) `requests` merges `params=` into the URL AFTER `Session.request` is entered, so the failure path saw a bare path — re-attached via `_with_params`, and the success path now reads the PREPARED url. (2) Far worse: `requests` embeds the full URL, key and all, in its exception messages ("Max retries exceeded with url: /v1/x?key=SECRET"). The path was redacted; the error text was not. Both were caught by one assertion — *no key appears anywhere in a recorded row* — which is why that is a test and not a comment. A third pass widened the scrubber to bare `token=…` with no leading `?`.
- **Odds API credits are the DELTA of `x-requests-used`** between consecutive responses, tracked per process. First call in a process records NULL rather than a guess; a negative delta (billing reset) records NULL too.
- **`curl_cffi` needed its own patch.** Master moved 4 commits ahead mid-session with a DK freshness collector on curl_cffi — a separate HTTP stack (browser TLS fingerprint) a requests patch cannot reach. Without it that feed would have recorded nothing, and *a feed that records nothing looks identical to a feed that is down* — the exact confusion this dashboard exists to remove.
- **Security: the server refuses to bind anything but loopback without `MONITOR_TOKEN`** (tested), since a Railway domain is public and the dashboard shows pipeline internals. Token compared with `compare_digest`; accepted as a query param because EventSource cannot set headers. `api_call_log` is RLS-on/no-policy with anon REVOKEd by name.
- **The dashboard was rendered in Chromium and looked at** (the dataviz skill's last step), which caught four defects review had not: a `.cr` class collision painting **credit counts in the critical red**, an inflated calls/min on connect (the snapshot seeded every historical call at "now" instead of its real timestamp), a favicon 404 on every load, and an unclamped negative latency bar.
- **Verification.** 27 new tests, all passing. Full suite **1,178 passed / 0 failed**; the `origin/master` baseline in a detached worktree is **1,163 passed / 1 failed** (the documented worktree-path assertion in `test_nfl_opener`) — zero regressions. Every read query was run against **production Postgres** via the Supabase MCP (pick counts, recent picks, runs, health, quota) and the `api_call_log` aggregates were validated against real Postgres through a `VALUES` CTE, since the MCP is read-only and cannot create the table. `test_db_setup` 7/7 with the new table in `EXPECTED_TABLES`.
- **Fixed a nightly flake found while verifying the deploy** (`test_discord_notifier.py::test_a_signal_says_when_it_was_written`, pre-existing on master — reproduced there before touching anything). It built a fixture 90 minutes in the past and asserted a bare `posted 10:32 PM ET`, so once the ET date rolled over the renderer correctly prefixed the date (`posted Sat 8/29, …`) and the assertion failed — **every night between midnight and ~2am ET**. The product behaviour is the session-137 design and is right; the test was wrong. Now: `_freeze_now` pins the module clock (only `_posted_et` reads it on this path — `_new_signals` takes an explicit date), the expectations are LITERALS rather than re-derived with the same `strftime` the code uses, and the two branches are asserted separately — same stamp read at 23:59 (bare time, and `8/29` asserted ABSENT so an always-prefix regression cannot pass) and at 00:02 (date prefix required). `test_a_signal_posted_on_an_earlier_day_carries_its_date` was pinned too: it could not flake, but it mirrored the implementation.
- **Verified by shifting the wall clock, not by reasoning.** A throwaway pytest plugin re-points the notifier's `datetime.now`; on the UNFIXED code at 00:02 ET it reproduces the exact failure, and on the fixed code the full suite passes at 00:02, 00:00:01, 23:59:59, midday, January (EST) and the ambiguous DST fall-back hour. Then mutation-tested the renderer both directions: removing the date prefix fails 2 tests, always prefixing fails 2 different ones — so the pair cannot be satisfied by a loosened substring. Full suite **1,208 passed** (master: 1,206 passed + that 1 failure; +1 net test).
- **Matt's two Railway steps (nothing works until these):** set `MONITOR_TOKEN` to a long random string, then the `worker` service → Settings → Networking → **Generate Domain**. Open `https://<domain>/?token=<TOKEN>`. Locally: `python -m monitoring`. The table self-creates on the first flush after deploy — `data/migrations/add_api_call_log.sql` is the reviewable copy, not a required step.

**Session summary (2026-08-29, session 134 — NCAAF live loop polls state every 10s (was 45s), on split cadences):**
- Matt: "I need the NCAAF api to run every 10-15 seconds for the live betting." Branch `claude/ncaaf-api-polling-live-betting-mk7xgz`.
- **The ancestor settled the design.** `nfl/live_model` — which `ncaaf_live/` was ported from — already runs **state at 10s and odds at 60s** with a deadline-based sleep. The NCAAF port collapsed both into one `time.sleep(45)`, which throttled the FREE, fast-moving feed to the speed of the METERED one. So this restores a split cadence rather than picking a new number: state **10s**, odds **15s**, both env-overridable (`NCAAF_LIVE_POLL_STATE_SEC` / `NCAAF_LIVE_POLL_ODDS_SEC`) plus `--interval` / `--odds-interval`.
- **Credits are not the constraint, and I checked the live figure rather than the comment** (the §1b rule): **4,359,858 remaining** of the 5M plan, typical day ~5k. At the 15s odds cadence a 12-hour Saturday is ~2,880 bulk fetches × ~4 credits ≈ **11.5k**, i.e. 0.26% of remaining; a full 15-week season ≈ 173k (4%). Cost does NOT scale in the number of live games — both feeds are one bulk call per pass — which is what makes a fast cadence affordable on a 60-game Saturday.
- **Four things would have broken had I only changed the constant**, and each is the actual content of this change:
  1. **The sleep was flat, not a deadline.** `time.sleep(45)` ran AFTER the work, so the true cadence was always interval + feed time. At 45s that error was ~10%; at 10s it dominates. Now sleeps the remainder (`max(0.0, interval - elapsed)`), and **warns when a pass overruns** so a feed-bound loop is visible instead of drifting silently. Verified end-to-end against the real `main()` with a stubbed 2s feed: steady-state sleeps **exactly 8.00s** at a 10s interval (old code: 12s).
  2. **The session credit cap was silently sized for 60s.** A flat `SESSION_CREDIT_CAP = 5000` fits ~720 fetches; at 15s it is exhausted mid-afternoon — and the old code then returned the CACHED payload forever, so the loop would have gone on pricing a line frozen hours earlier. Cap now sized in config (30k) **and** an `LIVE_ODDS_MAX_AGE_SEC` (180s) age bound added, so hitting the cap — or any sustained feed outage — reports NO odds and the engine declines, rather than betting a frozen number. Mirrors the platform's own `LIVE_ODDS_MAX_AGE_SEC`.
  3. **ESPN's per-game fan-out made 10s unreachable.** One summary call PER live game, sequentially, with a 0.2s politeness gap: 20 games × (~0.3s + 0.2s) ≈ 10s of a 10s budget before any pricing. Extracted `resolve_live_states()` — drops games the platform has no row for FIRST (an unknown matchup never costs a fetch), then fans the rest out over a small pool (`SUMMARY_FETCH_WORKERS`, 6). CFBD is untouched by this: it carries every game's state in its single scoreboard call, which is why the Railway source scales to any cadence.
  4. **A rate-limited feed looked exactly like an empty slate.** A failed fetch returns `[]` just like "no games are live", and the loop EXITS ~30 min after the last live game. Polling 4.5× faster makes a 429 likelier, so that conflation would have become: CFBD refuses → loop decides the slate is over → exits → supervisor restarts it → refuses again. Now only an ANSWERED feed advances the idle clock; an unanswered one holds and says so. Verified at runtime, not just in source.
- Also: CFBD's `fetch_scoreboard_cfbd` timeout cut 60s→15s. Its 2×60s retry budget was patience bought against a 45s re-poll; at 10s that same patience stalls the loop for up to two minutes, and a scoreboard that takes a minute to arrive describes a state that has already moved on. Fail fast — the next pass, seconds away, is the retry.
- **The one thing to watch (flagged, not fixed):** CFBD rate limits. 10s state polling is ~4,300 scoreboard calls over a 12-hour Saturday vs ~960 before. I could not verify CFBD's limit from the sandbox (blocked). If it starts refusing, raise `NCAAF_LIVE_POLL_STATE_SEC` in Railway Variables — env-only, no code change — and the loop now survives the refusal rather than exiting.
- **Verification:** NEW `ncaaf_live/tests/test_gameday_cadence.py` (16 tests: cadence band + independence + env override, cap sized against the configured cadence, deadline/overrun/odds-cadence source tripwires, the odds age bound in all four states — fresh, capped-but-fresh, capped-and-stale, sustained outage — and the fan-out: unknown games cost no fetch, parallel states stay ALIGNED to their games, CFBD does zero per-game work, one failed summary doesn't drop the others). Deps were installable in-sandbox so this actually ran: **full suite 1,103 passed / 6 failed, failure set byte-identical to the clean tree** (stash-compared: 1 documented `test_default_thresholds` drift + 5 environmental `models.scorer` import errors). Plus two runtime harnesses against the real `main()` — the 8.00s steady-state cadence above, and the loop holding through an unanswered feed.
- **Merged with #267 mid-session, which had landed on master in parallel and ALSO moved NCAAF live to 10s.** It changed the constant only (`POLL_SECONDS = 45 -> 10`), so the four structural items above are all still load-bearing — most importantly the deadline sleep: with a flat sleep the real cadence is 10s PLUS feed time, and ESPN's sequential per-game fan-out on a 20-game slate is itself ~10s, so master alone would have run at ~20s while reading 10s. Both sides' work is preserved (its first-signal repair at startup and its `or locked` notifier gate; my cadence/fan-out/idle-clock changes).
- **The one genuine divergence from #267, flagged rather than quietly overridden:** its comment states that tightening the poll "cannot increase Odds API spend", which was true because the priced bulk call debounced on a hard-coded 60s clock. Making the odds clock SETTABLE (default 15s) makes it a spend decision — sized and bounded accordingly (cap + age bound). That sentence was rewritten rather than kept, since it would otherwise be false. **`NCAAF_LIVE_POLL_ODDS_SEC=60` restores #267's exact behaviour with no code change** if Matt prefers it.
- **CFBD IS METERED, and the loop was billing it around the clock — found while answering "can I drop to the CFBD free tier?".** No: CFBD's free tier is **1,000 calls per calendar MONTH** (Patreon $1/5k, $5/30k, $10/75k + GraphQL), and CFBD is not retraining-only — `/scoreboard` + `/teams/fbs` are the LIVE STATE SOURCE on the Railway worker (site.api.espn.com is 403 there), plus the weekly in-season `step_ncaaf_stats`/`step_ncaaf_results`. Retraining is the *largest* user, not the only one.
- **The real find: idle burn.** `scheduler.py` claimed "idle invocations cost one CFBD call", but `gameday.py` initialises `last_live` at STARTUP and polls a full `IDLE_EXIT_MINUTES` (30) before exiting — after which the `*/10` supervisor relaunches it. So it billed CFBD at ~86% duty cycle 11am-midnight **whether or not any game was live**. At the old 45s that was ~27k calls/month (Tier 2, just fitting); **the 10s change would have taken it to ~120k/month, past even the largest published tier (75k)** — i.e. this PR would have broken NCAAF live by quota, silently, mid-season. This is the CFBD risk the PR body flagged as unverifiable; it is now measured, and it was worse than flagged.
- **Fixed two ways, measured 120,343 -> 17,850 calls/month** (fits Tier 2, $5, with headroom): (1) `POLL_IDLE_SEC` (60s) — the fast cadence buys reaction time to a live scoring drive, and there is nothing to react to when nothing is live; (2) **an immediate exit when nothing is live and the next kickoff is more than `IDLE_EXIT_MINUTES` away**, handing the wait back to the free `*/10` supervisor. (2) is behaviour the module docstring has always PROMISED ("exits when ... none starts within the lookahead") but never implemented — the code only ever checked time-since-live, so a loop launched hours early sat polling a paid API to learn what its own `ctx_map` already knew. `seconds_to_next_kickoff` returns None for unknown/unparseable, and every caller treats unknown as "stay up" — being wrong about a kickoff must never cost a game. The scheduler's idle-cost comment is now accurate rather than aspirational.
- **Delivery: NCAAF live runs on the Railway worker** (`ncaaf_live_loop` in `scheduler.py`, CFBD source — site.api is 403 there), so the faster cadence applies at the next redeploy after merge. Matt's local `gameday.py` runs pick it up on a `git pull`.

**Session summary (2026-08-29, session 133 — live picks lock at first BET signal (MLB + NCAAF) + "Locked" indicator):**
- Matt (from the live NCAAF board): the TCU-UNC live total showed Over 45.5, then Over 46.5 — "which one if any is a locked signal bet that counts towards the model record?" Answer at the time: NEITHER. Both live loops (`models/live_scorer.py`, `ncaaf_live/gameday.py`) delete-and-replaced every unsettled live row each pass (45s NCAAF / per-pass MLB), so a live BET was re-priced continuously and — worse — the NCAAF totals lane closing in Q4 (`TOTAL_MIN_SECONDS=900`) ERASED any standing pick before it could settle: live NCAAF totals bets could never reach the model record at all. Verified live mid-slate: the same Over 45.5 -120 row was rewritten with a new pick_id 46s apart. Matt's ruling: "if it fits a criteria, it should be locked. Most bets can post, but we should track bets that become signals." Branch `claude/ncaaf-live-bet-lockup-1zcds8`.
- **NEW `config.LOCK_LIVE_PICKS_AT_FIRST_SIGNAL` (default on, env "0" reverts):** the first live BET per (game, model) lane is the bet of record — locked at its line and price. Locked lanes are excluded from later passes' deletes AND inserts, so the locked row survives lane closes (Q4/OT), edge decay, and line moves, and settles through the existing generic game path (`_market_for_pick` via LIVE_MODELS; totals vs `scored_line`). Unlocked lanes keep churning — the board posts freely, only signals lock. Per-LANE, not per-side (one live totals bet per game, no middling); the complementary AVOID written in the locking pass freezes with its BET. Third member of the lock family (game 7am / prop first-signal / live first-BET).
- **Plumbing:** shared `_locked_live_lanes(conn, game_id, model_ids)` in `models/scorer.py` (lanes with an unsettled `is_live` BET); MLB write extracted into testable `_write_live_picks` (per-lane deletes, portable SQL); NCAAF `write_picks` consults the lock before its delete + insert. Both module docstrings + `docs/live_betting.md` updated.
- **Mobile indicator:** live BET rows are by definition locked now, so `PickCard` shows a green lock chip — "Locked Q1 — bet of record" (NCAAF period) / "Locked inning 3" (MLB) — exempt from the 2-chip hero cap (like injury: the user must know the number is frozen from first cross, not the churning live line). PickDetail live-track copy updated ("Live signals lock at the first BET"). Side benefit: locked rows keep a STABLE pick_id (never rewritten), de-churning live tracking's settled-row resolution.
- **Verification:** deps installed in-sandbox (pytest/numpy/pandas/xgboost/psycopg2) so this actually ran: NEW `tests/test_live_lock.py` (8) + `ncaaf_live/tests/test_gameday_lock.py` (6, incl. the exact 45.5→46.5 re-price scenario and the Q4 empty-pass erase) — all 14 pass, and the 6 gameday tests correctly FAIL against stashed base (they detect the bug). Full live suites: 82 passed / 9 errors, errors identical on base (LiveEngine needs Matt's-machine model artifacts — environmental). `npx tsc --noEmit` = 28 errors, **byte-identical to base** (diffed, the documented queries.ts casts); both touched mobile files parse through babel-preset-expo.
- **Delivery:** MLB side applies at the next Railway redeploy after merge; NCAAF applies when Matt PULLS before his next gameday.py run (it runs on his machine). Mobile chip ships via the auto-OTA on merge. Today's TCU-UNC live rows predate the lock — they churn/erase under the old rules; first locked live bets appear next slate.

**Session summary (2026-08-29, session 132 — NCAAF was invisible because scoring was CRASHING; plus "watching" rows so the board is never empty):**
- Matt: "When will the NCAAF betting lines start showing? I think we should be showing them now but only show the signals when they appear." The answer turned out to be two separate faults with one symptom. Branch `claude/ncaaf-betting-lines-timing-upw8xs`.
- **P0, and it was not an NCAAF outage — it was a TOTAL one.** `ncaaf_spread_premium` was registered, trained (2026-08-28) and added to `config.MODELS`, but never to `FEATURE_MAP`. `score_game` resolves `FEATURE_MAP[model_id]` BEFORE it looks at the artifact, so it raised `KeyError: 'ncaaf_spread_premium'` straight out of `run_scorer` — killing **game-level scoring for every sport, on every pass, all day**. Evidence: `pipeline_log` carries the bare KeyError on each pass since 10:00Z, and today's picks are **prop rows only** (a different step) — zero `mlb_moneyline`, zero `mlb_over_under`, 444 MLB picks against 2,864 yesterday. It stayed hidden overnight because 8/28's late passes had target_date 8/28, by which time every NCAAF game had started and was skipped — the first pass with an unstarted NCAAF game was this morning's.
- **Why it produced no alarm anyone would read as "NCAAF":** `refresh_pass_steps` correctly went CRIT ("scoring failing in ALL of the last 3 passes"), but the user-visible symptom was an empty NCAAF board, which is indistinguishable from a sport that simply has no picks.
- **Fixes: the entry, and the blast radius.** `FEATURE_MAP` gains the premium tier (it shares `NCAAF_SPREAD_FEATURES` — same rule, same market, disjoint |dev| band). The per-(game, model) call in `run_scorer` is now wrapped: a model that raises is logged, counted and skipped, the surviving picks are **committed**, and only then is a summary `RuntimeError` raised — so one broken model can never again take out four other sports, and the step still fails loudly rather than going quietly green.
- **The ask itself: a decline now writes a row.** Both NCAAF rules answered a game they would not bet with `return []`, so the game never appeared. Now every precondition failure returns a reason, the game surfaces with DK's live number, and the row is **forced to NONE** before insert. Forcing it explicitly rather than trusting the thresholds is load-bearing: the totals ECDF is asymmetric (OOS residual mean -0.62), so an inside-the-gate game can hand the UNDER side a probability above the 0.65 floor and would otherwise fire a BET the walk-forward never validated.
- **The opener rule moved into `_opener_rule`** (module-level, unit-testable), returning `(home_prob, away_prob, no_signal)`. Two declines still write nothing at all: no current DK line (nothing to display), and the band ceiling (the sibling tier is betting that exact side, and a "no signal" row beside its BET would read as the tiers contradicting each other).
- **Look-ahead + a signal-aware lock (new `docs/sports/ncaaf.md` section).** `NCAAF_SCORE_AHEAD_DAYS=7`; `locked_pairs` excludes NCAAF **NONE** rows so a watching row refreshes each pass while a real signal still locks at first cross. Without that exclusion a Monday NONE row would freeze the game for the week — and the totals rule, which is game-day by design, could never fire at all. `NCAAF_TOTALS_MAX_LEAD_DAYS=1` keeps totals firing on game day (validated against the archive's stored line, not an opener a week out); the opener rule needs no limit because its own preconditions are its window.
- **Mobile:** `fetchUpcomingNcaafPicks` (7-day window, signals ordered before NONE so the row cap can never drop a BET, all-books scoped by the `NCAAF_%` game_id prefix) merged into `useTodayPicks`. NCAAF is deliberately NOT a "preview" sport — `UNLOCKED_LOOKAHEAD_SPORTS` is empty by design and a fired NCAAF pick is a locked bet of record.
- **Verification — the suite actually ran this time.** PyPI turned out to be reachable from the sandbox (pytest, numpy, pandas, xgboost, optuna, psycopg2-binary, bs4, MLB-StatsAPI all installed), so this is not the usual "run it on your machine" hand-off: **branch = 1 failed / 1,011 passed; base (origin/master 93ae6d9) = 1 failed / 995 passed** — the same single pre-existing failure (`test_config.py::test_default_thresholds`, 0.1 vs 0.03), +16 new passing tests, zero regressions. New `tests/test_ncaaf_board_visibility.py` (16 tests); the headline one is generic: **every model in `config.MODELS` must have a `FEATURE_MAP` entry** — the bug class, not this instance. `tests/test_ncaaf_cross_book_opener.py` repointed at the helper, and its two `return []`-counting assertions rewritten for the new semantics (a decline returns the neutral 0.5 + a reason, and must never hand back the artifact's validated win rate). `npx tsc --noEmit` = **28 errors, byte-identical to master** (stash-compared pre-commit).
- **`tests/test_feature_engine.py::TestFeatureMap::test_all_models_present` had to be updated — and it is worth knowing why it did not catch this.** It asserts `FEATURE_MAP.keys()` equals a HAND-MAINTAINED set, so it guards against a stray key but is blind to a model registered with no features: the missing entry and the missing expectation are the same omission. It now carries the premium tier plus a pointer to the derived (`config.MODELS`-based) invariant that would have caught it.
- **Two methodology corrections worth carrying forward.** (1) `git stash` does NOT give you a master baseline once your work is COMMITTED — it only reverts uncommitted changes, and a stash-based comparison after committing silently compares the branch to itself. Use `git worktree add --detach <base>`. (2) Local `master` in these sandboxes can be far behind (**50 commits** here) — diff against `origin/master`, not `master`. Related: the "24 failures on master" figure repeated through recent session summaries is an artifact of missing optional deps; with the full set installed, origin/master has exactly **one**.
- **Nothing recovers until this MERGES** (the worker runs master): every pass until then still scores no game-level picks for any sport. After merge + redeploy, expect the `refresh_pass_steps` CRIT to clear and NCAAF rows to appear the same pass.
- **[RESOLVED 2026-08-30 — see session 139 below.]** ~~**Flagged, NOT fixed — NCAAF games are duplicated across two date conventions.**~~ The odds ingestor dates a game by its **ET** kickoff; `cfbd_ingestor.parse_games` uses `start_date[:10]`, which is **UTC**. A Saturday-night kick therefore gets two `games` rows under two game_ids (15 duplicated matchups in 2026 so far, and it grows as evening games get priced). Consequence: odds — and therefore picks — attach to the `live` row while CFBD writes scores to the `cfbd` row, so **an evening NCAAF pick may never settle**. Not fixed here because correcting the derivation re-keys existing rows and orphans `ncaaf_team_game_log` / `ncaaf_qb_game`. The cheap mitigation is the pattern UFC already uses: match results by team pair ±1 day.

And currently it's showing 2 sections with nothing selected. The default should be nothing. Then the user can add bets to a bet slip or remove them completely." Mobile-only; no DB/pipeline/scorer/threshold/model changes, no new deps. Branch `claude/remove-bet-slip-features-vd6qxr`.

With them gone the screen renders exactly one thing — the slip — so the mode toggle, the sport toggle (the slip is cross-sport), the Legs/Style/Target-odds panel, the alternatives list and the leg-swap sheet all went with them. The custom-leg form survives, collapsed from three modes (`add`/`swap`/`manual-add`) to one.

A slip holding only selections that no longer resolve (yesterday's picks, a scratched player) therefore showed `Betslip (2)` over "Your betslip is empty" — and the "N selections no longer available · tap to clear" note lived **inside the non-empty branch**, so the one affordance that could clear them was unreachable in exactly the state that produced them. The note is now computed once and rendered in both branches, so a phantom badge is always clearable.

The screen now shows a spinner while the slip is unread, or while it has selections and today's picks haven't landed — but NOT when the slip is genuinely empty, where "empty" is the correct immediate answer.

Every remaining export was confirmed to still have a consumer first. ParlayScreen 1,954 → 1,107 lines; parlay.ts 812 → 588. 35 orphaned style keys removed (tsconfig has no `noUnusedLocals`, so nothing else would have caught them).
- **Deliberately kept:** the correlation engine (`computeCorrelatedMetrics` still prices the slip — correlation is why a same-game slip's true joint probability differs from the naïve product), `BetslipButton` on the Picks header (still the way into an empty slip, to review/clear selections or reach Saved), and `Saved` parlays incl. restore.

**Session summary (2026-08-29, session 132 — filter sheet rebuilt as a compact bottom sheet of collapsed rows):**
- Matt (screenshot of a competitor's stats filter): "I like how simple it is and the UI of it. Let's do something similar." Mobile-only; no DB/pipeline/scorer/threshold/model changes, no new deps. Branch `claude/stats-page-filter-ui-faelso`.
- **The simplicity in the reference is COLLAPSED ROWS, not new controls.** Ours already had the same filters, but rendered every control group expanded in a full-screen `pageSheet` — six open sections you scroll through. Theirs is a short bottom sheet where each filter is one row (`Games ⌄`, `Period ⌄`), so the whole filter surface fits above the fold and "what can I filter on, and what is set right now" is one glance.
- **`FilterSection` gains an optional `summary`** — pass it and the row collapses to `title … value ⌄`; omit it and the section renders expanded exactly as before, so the change is opt-in per section. The summary is the value in the same words the row uses when open, which is what makes a collapsed row still worth reading (`Sort by … Hit rate`, `Qualifiers … 29+ games · auto`, `Hit rate … 60%+`).
- **`FilterSheet` is now a bottom sheet** (transparent modal + backdrop + grabber + `maxHeight: 85%`), reusing the established `SportsbookPickerSheet` chrome, with **"Clear all"** moved to the header where the reference puts it.
- **One deliberate departure: no Close + Apply footer.** Session 112 standardized this sheet on live editing, so Apply has nothing to commit and a second button that also just closes is a decision for no reason. The single result-count button (`Show 24 players`) stays — it reports the outcome instead of asking for a confirmation.
- **Two bugs the new layout would have introduced, both fixed:** (1) a bottom-anchored sheet puts the search box and number fields under the keyboard — wrapped in `KeyboardAvoidingView` as the OUTER flex:1 element, since the sheet's percentage `maxHeight` only resolves against a parent with a definite height (the session-53 parlay-modal lesson); (2) a Modal keeps its children mounted while hidden, so rows would have remembered their expanded state and `defaultOpen` would never have fired — a generation key bumped only when the sheet OPENS remounts the rows, so it always presents collapsed and nothing collapses mid-dismiss.
- **Applied to the Picks sheet too** (Signal / Market / Minimums summaries), because the whole point of session 112 was one filter system — leaving Picks as a long expanded scroll while Stats is a collapsed list would fork the mental model again.
- **`bandSummary` is now one definition** shared by the collapsed hit-rate row and the removable pill, so the two can't describe the same band differently (the pill now reads `hit 60%+` / `hit 60–80%` / `hit ≤ 80%`, matching the preset chips instead of the old `hit ≥ 60%`).
- **The tonight/next-slate toggle deliberately stays on the front page** even though the reference has "Playing Today" inside the sheet — session 123 moved it out precisely so it wasn't buried, and visible beats buried.
- **Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` = **28 errors, error set byte-identical to master** (stash-compared; the documented `queries.ts` casts + the pre-existing `verify_player_log` fixture), 0 in touched files.
